| KI-0045 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **The one-shot timer is re-armed only from paths the timer itself drives, so once a system goes fully idle nothing ever wakes it again — a SPLIT SOURCE OF TRUTH between `sched::timers::runtime::ARMED_NS` and the hardware.** `ARMED_NS[cpu]` records what the scheduler believes it programmed; the arch side (`arch_irq::deadline::program`) writes `IA32_TSC_DEADLINE` only `if crate::lapic::timer_deadline_mode()` and has NO else branch, so a failed arm leaves the scheduler believing a deadline is pending that the hardware will never deliver. Nothing outside the timer path recomputes it, so the state is unrecoverable: the tick would re-arm the tick. Same circular shape as the ktimers wedge fixed by B1348. | F1263, measured by GDB on a frozen guest with a desktop up. After **275 s** of uptime: `ARMED_NS[0]=0x74f44006` (1.962 s) and `ARMED_NS[1]=0x7515cfbc` (1.964 s) — the last arm the scheduler recorded is **273 seconds in the past** — `NEXT_TICK_NS` identical, and `arch_irq::lapic::dispatch::TICK_COUNT=4`: the LAPIC timer has fired four times since boot. Both CPUs halted with **IF SET** (`eflags 0x282` / `0x246`), one in `cpuidle::idle::generic::enter`, so they are waiting for an interrupt that is never programmed, not spinning and not masked. `TSC_KHZ=0x4017b7` (4.2 GHz), so the clock source is fine. **Pre-existing, not live-image specific:** a stock ext4 `gnome` boot of the same kernel shows `TICK_COUNT=3` frozen across 25 s too — it survives only because continuous I/O keeps generating interrupts. The live image exposes it because it actually reaches idle. User-visible as a desktop that draws, runs, and then locks solid: JWM's clock stops advancing, X's cursor stops moving (X draws it, so a frozen guest freezes the pointer), and the guest burns 1 s of CPU per 70 s of wall clock. | unowned |

| KI-0100 | OPEN | MISSING | med | **ext4's multiblock allocator is still only a subset of Linux mballoc.** Fresh multi-block `fallocate` and fresh multi-block `write_at` requests reserve one contiguous run and prefer a stripe-aligned start, so `stripe=` affects real production allocation paths. B2816 adds a validated per-mount block-bitmap cache, matching the resident bitmap half of Linux's buddy state; B2817 adds a validated largest-free-order summary for groups already loaded by this mount, so optimized scans can prefer a group with a suitably large free extent. B2822 adds reusable locality-group data preallocation for small regular-file writes, with disk-free tails masked in the in-memory bitmap and consumed by another file in the same group. B2823 makes regular-file append consume an existing inode PA first and a locality-group PA second. B2824 makes a fresh aligned regular-file append retain an inode-owned PA tail; directory and symlink append remain one-block allocations. B2825 batches the two metadata blocks needed by an inline-root extent split into one atomic metadata allocation request. B2826 batches all replacement leaf metadata blocks when a punch/truncate rebuilds a depth-1 extent tree. B2827 adds Linux's average-free-fragment order summary and uses it to prioritize loaded groups that can satisfy a multiblock request. B2828 adds an order-indexed group set so suitable loaded groups are selected without scanning every group. B2829 adds the companion largest-free-order index for power-of-two requests. B2830 applies Linux's size-based regular-file preallocation growth through the 16 KiB/power-of-two phase and bounded larger-window fallback. **B2850 makes locality preallocation ownership per CPU, matching Linux's `s_locality_groups[smp_processor_id()]` and carrying the owner through claim after blocking operations. B2852 adds inode-hashed stream/global-goal group reuse for large regular-file allocations, recording the actual successful group in canonical mount allocator state. B3004 adds indexed candidate scanning, B3005 restores physical-goal-first ordering, B3006 orders locality-PA reclaim by filesystem group, and B3007 filters PA masking to the target group.** The remaining gap is buddy/bitmap preallocation lifecycle transitions and request normalization; the extent-tree insertion sites that allocate one metadata block at a time are reference-shaped and are not a missing batch. | B2816, B2817, B2820, B2822, B2823, B2824, B2825, B2826, B2827, B2828, B2829, B2830, B2850, B2852, B3004, B3005, B3006, B3007. `MountState::block_bitmap_cache`, `group_free_order`, `group_free_order_index`, `group_avg_fragment_order`, and the order-indexed group set are updated only through successful allocation/free paths; `balloc_image`, `balloc_uninit_e2fsck`, all ext4 unit tests, all ext4 image suites, and both-arch boot smoke pass. Linux `fs/ext4/mballoc.c` remains the reference for the remaining mballoc lifecycle and normalization. | ext4 lane |

| KI-0205 | OPEN | MISSING | blocker | **Native PCI xHCI now exists and is registered before PCI driver probing:** class `0x0c0330`, IOMMU-mapped DMA pages, controller reset/run, MSI/MSI-X binding, root-port address/configure, USB HID boot keyboard/mouse reports, platform-keyed evdev publication, and root-port connect/disconnect handling through the USB softirq. The old claim that no xHCI or bus-agnostic input existed is false. USB mass-storage descriptor discovery, Bulk-Only/SCSI CBW/CSW contracts, generic bulk endpoint-context construction, retained requester-keyed bulk DMA owners, non-overwriting transfer-completion handoff, native configuration sequencing, native CBW/data/CSW transfer submission, initial INQUIRY/READ CAPACITY transaction sequencing, canonical `sd*` block-device publication, and page-vector payload transfers now exist. One transaction can map up to 255 IOMMU DMA pages and submits chained Normal TRBs with a completion only on the terminal buffer. Root addressing now flows through an xHCI topology owner that preserves a physical root port plus route string. Hub-class status endpoints are parsed, configured, submitted, completion-validated, DMA-invalidated, and rearmed through that same transport owner; the hub descriptor is fetched in two stages, then its protocol/TT facts update the xHCI hub slot context. Hub initialization powers every downstream port, waits its one descriptor-provided power-good interval, reads changed/initial port status in a process worker, acknowledges change features, resets connected ports, and publishes descendant slots with the parent route string. The port reset-control transaction drops controller/device locks before completion waits and recovery delay. Descendant detach and recursive topology are still absent. The adapter owns the SCSI disk reservation until detach, rejects reuse when the canonical registry cannot detach an open disk, and serializes every command through the controller-then-device transaction gate. The shared block registry owns the reusable SCSI `sd*` identity allocator, so SATA and USB storage do not publish through competing counters; a shared SCSI midlayer, full hub topology, HID report-descriptor support beyond boot protocol, and physical xHCI exercise remain. The Linux module facade remains a shim and is not the native-driver path. | `drv-xhci::{probe,probe_hub,storage_block,irq,completion,usb,storage,context,hid,device}`; `block::{registry,BlockDevice}`; `pci-boot::register_pci_model_drivers`; `input::{InputDeviceKey::platform,install,publish_evdev}`; `irq::take_port_changes` → `probe::service_port_changes`. | B2111-xhci-linux-audit; B2112-usb-mass-storage; B211301-xhci-bulk-endpoints; B2113-xhci-storage-dma; B2114-xhci-completion-queue; B2115-xhci-storage-probe; B2116-xhci-bot-execution; B2118-block-sd-name-allocator; B2119-xhci-controller-ownership; B2120-xhci-controller-handle; B2121-xhci-storage-transaction; B2122-xhci-storage-block-device; B2124-xhci-storage-scatter-gather; B2125-xhci-hub-topology; B2126-xhci-hub-endpoint; B2127-xhci-hub-port-control; B2128-xhci-hub-port-status; B2129-xhci-hub-child-enumeration |

| KI-0267 | OPEN | COVERAGE | MED | The `[WATCHDOG] soft lockup: no reschedule for 10s on tid=4555 (Mutter Input Th) last_syscall=read` in `boot.txt` has NO established mechanism. Ruled OUT by reading: the evdev `read_file` retry loop was suspected of spinning past `schedule()` via its `park -> cancel_current_park -> continue` branch, but `has_pending()` (`is_empty()` -> `ready == 0`) and `try_pop_bytes()` (`count = min(dst/EVENT, ready)`, `None` iff `count == 0`) gate on the SAME `ready` field under the same lock, and the only other `None` paths (`revoked`/`disconnected`) are caught by `!is_live()`, which returns ENODEV at the top of the loop. Every exit terminates, so that loop is not the spin. What IS established: the kernel is not tick-preemptive in kernel mode (the timer IRQ only sets `need_resched`), and `charge_exec_runtime` runs only from `update_curr()` inside `schedule()` — which is why the victim shows 42ms cputime after a 10s stall, and why 'low cputime' must NOT be read as 'starved'. Still needed: identify what the task was actually executing. A separately observed guest pegging 99.9% host CPU after journald starts, unresponsive to userspace AND to kernel-level sysrq, may or may not be the same thing. Confound to eliminate first: the recorded case of a soft lockup manufactured by host memory contention. | `boot.txt:264-645`; `crates/drivers/drv-virtio-input/src/devfs/fileops.rs:64-82`; `crates/drivers/drv-virtio-input/src/evdev_queue.rs:107,183`; `crates/kernel/sched/src/diag/watchdog.rs:71-101`; `crates/kernel/sched/src/cputime.rs:72-76`; `crates/kernel/sched/src/live/schedule/switch.rs:2`. | unowned |

| KI-0272 | OPEN | MISSING | med | A bundled receive makes ONE pass over the run it mapped. The reference, when the transfer filled every mapped segment AND the description still reports more data immediately available, retries into the rest of the run and appends to the same completion. Nothing here reports "bytes still queued" out of a receive, so the condition that would trigger the second pass cannot be evaluated and the pass is never attempted. Not an ABI divergence — the reference takes the same single-pass branch whenever no more data is queued, and the completion is well-formed either way — but a caller receiving a burst gets more completions than it would on the reference. Closes when the receive path returns the queued-byte count alongside the transfer. | `io_uring::dispatch::bundle_io::run` returns after one `recvmsg::recv`; `io_uring_abi::bundle` has no retry decision to test because there is no input for one | io_uring lane (needs the net receive path to report queued bytes) |

| KI-0290 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | **Something decrements `preempt_count` once more than it incremented it, wrapping the count one below zero — the seed of the B2264 livelock, still unattributed.** The scheduler now repairs the count instead of livelocking on it, so the symptom is survivable, but the unpaired decrement is a real accounting bug: while the count is wrapped, `should_resched()` and `preemptible()` both read a non-zero word, so that CPU cannot take a reschedule at any preempt-enable until a `schedule()` repairs it. Intermittent: the F1241 post-merge x86 smoke named `local_bh_enable` subtracting `0x200` from zero at `sched/src/bh.rs`, while its retry and later diagnostic boots passed. | B2264 added the pre-subtract detector. F1242 established that the reported `bh.rs` line is the outer subtraction funnel, not an acquisition: `LockBhGuard::drop` had discarded its held-lock trace before reaching it. The pair is now checked while that trace is live, so a recurrence from `LockBhGuard` prints its exact `rank@file:line`. B2313 audited every production entry: core `lock_bh` users all funnel through `SchedBh`; the only raw pair was the Linux-module ABI, and the boot image contains no `.ko`; no unmatched direct `local_bh_enable` exists. The module ABI now gives raw spin locks Linux's preemption credit, returns it on failed trylock, and balances the combined `_bh` credit; this closes that parity gap but does not identify the intermittent underflow. The only production count replacements are the per-handler restore and B2264 scheduler recovery. Linux 7.2-rc4 has the same per-handler snapshot/restore, and B2264 preserves every count at or above the schedule-entry level, so neither can erase a live `0x200` credit in the observed path. Linux additionally tracks BH-disable depth on `current` and disables migration; this tree keeps only a per-CPU count, but no task switch or migration while the credit is live was found, so changing ownership would be speculation. Five independent B2313 x86 boots passed first attempt, userspace alive at 45–47 s, with no `[PREEMPT-LEAK]`, lost-credit, handler-repair, or `[BUG]` line. No exact acquisition recurred and no root-cause fix is claimed. | unowned |

What remains, and it is written in the engine itself: `run_completion_bottom_half`'s doc says *"The current request engine has one owner at a time... ## Ext4 master program

The authoritative dependency-ordered ext4 inventory and execution plan is [`scratch/ext4.md`](ext4.md). The older ext4 rows above retain historical measurements and are mapped to the IDs below; they are not a second completion list.

The synchronous metadata-read contract has no cancellation point: the owner waits for completion, publishes the result, then removes in-flight ownership. | metadata-owner tests cover the concurrent failed-owner window; real-root x86_64/AArch64 workloads, target checks, and current x86/AArch64 boot-smokes pass. The reference metadata path likewise waits uninterruptibly for the buffer completion. |

| E4-07 | DONE | Exact physical preallocation ownership, failed-claim rollback, contiguous locality-tail consumption with Linux-style size re-bucketing, Linux's `fls(len)-2` average-fragment buckets, the bounded three-order best-available scan fallback, bounded multiblock extent selection, and inode-PA release on truncate, punch-hole, range shifts, final orphan eviction, last-writer release, ENOSPC discard, and mount teardown are covered. | merged ownership/rollback/scan/lifecycle fixes; fragmentation, ENOSPC, migration, remount, and e2fsck-clean coverage. |
| E4-08 | OPEN | Htree selection improved and Linux RCU now removes much of the cached-walk overhead; the rwsem reader fast path avoids the wait-lock round trip for uncontended shared readers, delayed-allocation buffered writes no longer reread the inode table before returning, and linear-directory byte matching now avoids per-entry object and closure work while borrowing the shared metadata block. Linear scans retain Linux's per-directory last-successful-block hint. Special inode construction and directory mutation now reuse parsed inode fields rather than rereading the inode table. Create/mkdir/symlink/mknod and ordinary VFS unlink/rmdir/link/rename/writeback now durably publish journal records before returning and leave home checkpointing to the background owner, matching the reference journal-handle boundary. Victim-aware VFS unlink/rmdir now retain the already-resolved same-mount inode identity instead of rescanning the parent; direct name-only callers retain the fallback lookup. Victim-aware unlink also reuses the VFS inode type and avoids a duplicate pre-transaction inode read. Unlink/rmdir now retain the cached directory lookup image because those mutations do not change its size, flags, or inode generation. Ext4 inode xattrs now load lazily behind a sleepable per-inode owner on the first xattr, ACL, or list operation, matching Linux's on-demand path. Checkpoint backpressure makes the periodic owner skip a busy transaction gate and retry on the next tick. Rename now reuses its outer transaction for delete/rmdir/link-count/timestamp/dotdot/whiteout operations. Fresh create fanout attribution reached GNOME in three controlled SMP=1 runs: prepare/quota/post work stayed below 1 ms aggregate, create backend measured 1,604–2,596 ms, and transaction-gate accumulation reached 2,142 ms across mutators. Linux admits concurrent handles to one running transaction with protected metadata buffers and allocation-group state; this tree now releases the mount-wide gate during unrelated operation bodies and atomically retires locality-PA ownership at selection. Aggregate closure remains open at ~12–15x host; the blocking ext4 lookup path, residual mutation work, and repeatable controlled performance evidence remain the owners. |
| E4-08 status update | OPEN | Per-metadata-block writer ownership, per-handle rollback frames, allocation-group ownership, and gate release during unrelated operation bodies are implemented. Locality-PA selection retires its claimed prefix atomically under the mount state lock, preventing duplicate claims under concurrent handles. Canonical VFS regular-file create, mkdir, symlink, and mknod now reuse the locked parent inode image for inheritance and directory insertion, matching Linux's in-core ownership; noncanonical/test callers retain the explicit fallback. Syscall symlink/mknod callbacks now pass the already-resolved negative dentry proof through unchecked hooks, and fresh symlink inode bytes are carried forward instead of rereading the just-written inode; direct helpers remain checked. `mkdir` now advances the live VFS parent `i_nlink`, cached ext4 parent image, and serialized link count together, matching Linux `ext4_mkdir`/`inc_nlink`; its initial directory-block append also publishes the updated live extent image, fixing first-child creation after inode reuse. Commit `b1426119e` plus the current live-extent lane; the ext4 suite is 378/378; the create/readback harness is 8/8; ACL is 4/4; the allocator/e2fsck harness is 7/7; the real-root harness is 7/7, including systemd symlink walks and metadata/remount checks. This correctness change claims no aggregate speed gain. |

covered. Legacy indirect inodes preserve the legacy pointer tree for mapped

multi-block inline conversion and legacy-indirect mutation are covered by
canonical owners. Collapse/insert range remains extent-only, matching Linux;

provider and real-image mount evidence are complete. SMP=2 can still wedge

E4-10 status update (B2989): inline data is also converted before fallocate,
punch-hole, and truncate, so those operations cannot dispatch inline payload
bytes to the indirect-pointer owner. The focused inline-image operation test
and the full ext4 suite pass on merged main.

E4-10 status update (B2990): polled DIO now admits device-aligned offsets and
lengths, translates sub-filesystem-block ranges to the correct device sectors,
and delegates partial writes into unwritten extents to the synchronous RMW
owner. Deferred-device image coverage and the full ext4 suite pass.

E4-08 status update (B2994): linear directory lookup now reads the current
directory block through the canonical metadata owner before queuing only the
following bounded readahead window. This removes the prior duplicate extent
mapping walk for the current block and matches Linux's batch-read/consume
ordering. Focused and full ext4 tests plus both architecture checks pass; the
aggregate performance item remains open because the fresh GNOME-marker run did
not reach a valid desktop sample.

| E4-15 | DONE | The controlled GNOME/SMP=1 comparison and phase attribution are recorded. The result does not claim a whole-boot gain: the current run remains within the documented workload variance and attributes the dominant resolution cost to parent-lock waits, not inode-table lookup selection. | `scratch/perf-history.md` E4-15 perf-r1 and perf-r2; `target/perf-report-x86_64.log`; fresh-main repeatability run. |
| E4-16 | DONE | Historical ext4 ledger rows are explicitly historical, mapped to the E4 inventory, and no longer carry the stale pending SHA or old current-suite count. | `scratch/perf-history.md` E4-08 maps to merged `6da0ed7c0`; the superseded 62-failure row records the current 373-unit/full-image harness result. |

Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-02): the canonical NT object namespace now
owns named event creation/opening over the native event wait object. Named
events reuse one object identity across handles, report type collisions, and
are enumerated as Event entries; missing parent directories cannot be created.
The graph's `NtOpenEvent` boundary is now native rather than an explicit
unsupported result.

Windows NT frontier update: the Notepad import graph now resolves through
`NtAdjustGroupsToken`; its bounded replacement/reset path is implemented, while
previous-state/return-length buffers and `NtAdjustPrivilegesToken` remain open.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtAdjustPrivilegesToken` now has
bounded privilege mutation, disable-all, and prior-state output; the graph
advances to `ntdll.dll!NtAllocateLocallyUniqueId`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtAllocateLocallyUniqueId` now
validates the output pointer and allocates monotonic LUID values; the graph
advances to `ntdll.dll!NtAllocateVirtualMemoryEx`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtAllocateVirtualMemoryEx` now
accepts the native seven-argument ABI, validates the seventh stack argument,
and delegates zero-extended-parameter allocations to the existing VM owner;
the graph advances to `ntdll.dll!NtCancelIoFile`. Extended parameter records
remain unsupported until their VM semantics are implemented.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtCancelIoFile` and
`NtCancelIoFileEx` validate NT file handles and publish IO status blocks;
the synchronous NT file adapter has no pending request queue, so Ex-specific
request cancellation reports `STATUS_NOT_FOUND` and synchronous-I/O
cancellation remains open. The graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtCancelSynchronousIoFile`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtCancelSynchronousIoFile` now
validates current/real NT thread handles and publishes the reference
`STATUS_NOT_FOUND` result when no synchronous request is pending; the graph
advances to `ntdll.dll!NtCompareObjects`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtCompareObjects` now distinguishes
current-process/current-thread pseudo handles, validates process-local handles,
and compares duplicated handles by canonical NT object identity; the graph
advances to `ntdll.dll!NtConvertBetweenAuxiliaryCounterAndPerformanceCounter`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01):
`NtConvertBetweenAuxiliaryCounterAndPerformanceCounter` now exposes the native
four-argument ABI and the Wine-compatible unsupported-counter result (including
the required null-input access violation); the graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtCreateKey`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): the existing `CreateKey` service is
now reachable from the synthetic NTDLL export page as `NtCreateKey`; registry
key object semantics are not yet implemented, and the graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtCreateNamedPipeFile`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtCreateNamedPipeFile` now has an
explicit native export and an explicit `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` boundary;
named-pipe object and connection semantics remain pending. The graph advances
to `ntdll.dll!NtCreateSectionEx`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-05): `NtCreateSectionEx` now forwards
zero and nonzero extended-parameter arrays through the ordinary section owner;
the array is intentionally ignored, matching the native compatibility path.
The graph advances to its `ntdll.dll!NtCreateSymbolicLinkObject`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtCreateSymbolicLinkObject` now has
an explicit native export and fail-closed `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` boundary;
symbolic-link object semantics remain pending. The graph advances to its next
unresolved native import, `ntdll.dll!NtCreateUserProcess`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-02): `NtCreateUserProcess` now validates
the x86-64 attribute-list ABI, resolves the UTF-16 image name, prepares a
fresh PE address space with inherited Wine module catalog, installs typed
process/thread handles, and publishes the child only after PEB/TEB setup.
`CREATE_SUSPENDED` is represented through the native suspend depth and resumed
through the existing scheduler wake path. The normalized command line,
double-NUL environment, current directory, console handle, and standard
handles are now copied into the child-owned PEB. The complete 88-byte x64
`PS_CREATE_INFO` success union now returns the normalized parameter and PEB
pointers; section and manifest handles remain explicitly zero because those
owners are not yet present.
The graph advances to `ntdll.dll!NtDelayExecution`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtDelayExecution` now uses the
existing scheduler interruptible wait path with native relative/absolute
100-ns timeout conversion and alertable-result mapping; the graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtDeleteKey`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtDeleteKey` now has an explicit
native export and fail-closed `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` boundary; registry key
deletion semantics remain pending. The graph advances to its next unresolved
native import, `ntdll.dll!NtDeleteValueKey`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtDeleteValueKey` now has an
explicit native export and fail-closed `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` boundary;
registry value deletion semantics remain pending. The graph advances to its
next unresolved native import, `ntdll.dll!NtDuplicateToken`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtDuplicateToken` now validates
token-handle access, requested access, token type, and output memory, then
returns a new process-local handle retaining the token state. The graph
advances to `ntdll.dll!NtEnumerateKey`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-03): `NtEnumerateKey` and
`NtEnumerateValueKey` now route through the canonical userspace registry owner
with Wine-shaped basic/full/partial record encoding and access checks. The
remaining registry gap is hive load/save serialization, not enumeration.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-03): `NtFilterToken` now creates an
independent native token object, removes requested group SIDs, and disables
requested privileges. Unsupported flags and restricted-SID filtering remain
explicit until those token owners are defined. The graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtFlushBuffersFile`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtFlushBuffersFile` now resolves
through the native file adapter and flushes the backing VFS file with NT
handle-access and `IO_STATUS_BLOCK` completion semantics. The graph advances
to `ntdll.dll!NtFlushInstructionCache`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtFlushInstructionCache` now has
an explicit native export and validates the current-process handle before the
x86/x86_64 coherent-cache no-op. The graph advances to `ntdll.dll!NtFlushKey`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtFlushKey` now has an explicit
native export and fail-closed boundary while registry state remains owned by
the userspace registry service. The graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtFlushVirtualMemory`.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-01): `NtFlushVirtualMemory` now validates
the current-process scope, rounds and verifies the requested mapped range,
flushes file-backed VMA ranges through the canonical backing owner, and
publishes the adjusted address/size. `NtGetContextThread` now exposes the
current x86_64 task's integer/control context from the canonical `PtRegs`
owner; remote, debug, floating-point, segment, and XSTATE context remain
explicitly unsupported. The graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtGetNlsSectionPtr`, which now resolves Wine-compatible NLS data
files from `/usr/share/wine/nls` through the normal read-only file-backed VMA
path. `NtGetTickCount` now uses the monotonic timekeeper with Windows'
32-bit millisecond wrap semantics. The graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtGetWriteWatch`. The write-watch ABI is exposed with strict
current-process validation but remains `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` until VMA
and page-fault dirty tracking has a dedicated owner; the graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtImpersonateAnonymousToken`. Wine’s own implementation is a
`STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` stub, so Oxide exposes the same explicit boundary;
the graph advances to `ntdll.dll!NtIsProcessInJob`. That call now consults
the existing NT job association and returns Wine-compatible in-job status;
the graph advances to `ntdll.dll!NtLoadKey`. Its ABI/export are exposed, but
hive loading remains `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` until the userspace registry
owner gains a typed load transaction. The graph advances to
`ntdll.dll!NtLockVirtualMemory`.
`NtLockVirtualMemory` now follows Wine’s current-process path: it rounds and
publishes the range, applies the VMM-owned locked VMA state, and populates
resident pages through the PMM path. Remote-process locking remains an
explicit `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` boundary until the NT APC/process-memory
owner exists. The graph advances to the next unimplemented NT export.
The next graph export, `ntdll.dll!NtMakeTemporaryObject`, is implemented by
the canonical NT object namespace and handle lifetime owner. The graph then
reaches `ntdll.dll!NtMapViewOfSectionEx`; its current-process, zero-count
extended-parameter form now follows the ordinary section mapping owner,
including the reference rule that a non-null parameter pointer is ignored
when its count is zero. Nonzero extended-parameter decoding and remote-process
APC mapping remain explicit `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` boundaries until those
owners exist.
The graph now reaches `ntdll.dll!NtNotifyChangeDirectoryFile`; the supported
non-subtree create/delete watch path is backed by the VFS dirent observer and
an NT event. Watch records are owned by the directory handle and issuing TID,
`NtCancelIoFile[Ex]` completes matching records with `STATUS_CANCELLED`, and
handle close completes them with `STATUS_HANDLES_CLOSED`. Subtree and the
remaining notify filters, plus APC/completion-port delivery for these watches,
remain explicit work.
The graph then reaches `ntdll.dll!NtNotifyChangeKey`; the supported
non-subtree `REG_NOTIFY_CHANGE_LAST_SET` watch is now owned by the native key
object and issuing TID/IOSB, with cancellation and final-handle teardown
completion. APC delivery, subtree traversal, and the remaining filters remain
explicit work until their owners exist.
`NtOpenEvent` is now implemented through the canonical named-event namespace;
the earlier unsupported-boundary note is superseded by the 2026-09-02 update.
`NtQueryAttributesFile` is implemented through the canonical VFS resolver and
inode metadata owner, with Windows `FILE_BASIC_INFORMATION` time and type
translation.
`NtQueryDefaultLocale` is implemented with strict NT-personality/pointer
validation and the current en-US baseline LCID (`0x0409`).
`NtQueryDefaultUILanguage` is implemented against the same validated en-US
baseline (`LANGID 0x0409`).
`NtQueryDirectoryObject` is now implemented through the canonical object
directory namespace with restart/context and variable-record ABI handling.
`NtOpenProcess` now resolves cross-process NT `CLIENT_ID` values through the
canonical scheduler registry, normalizes a target to its process-group leader,
rejects Linux-personality targets, and installs a process-local handle with
validated access rights. Remote memory access remains a separate boundary.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-02): the canonical NT object namespace now
owns named semaphore creation and opening over the native scheduler semaphore.
Named handles reuse one object identity, report collision/type/missing-parent
statuses, and enumerate as `Semaphore` entries.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-02): named section creation publishes
existing anonymous or file-backed section objects into the canonical NT
namespace, while `NtOpenSection` resolves typed handles with validated access.
Section mapping continues through the existing VMM-backed view owner.
`NtOpenSemaphore` now resolves named semaphore handles through the canonical
NT object namespace; semaphore wait/release behavior remains scheduler-owned.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-04): NT symbolic-link objects now have a
canonical target owner with create/open/query services, typed access checks,
Unicode target copy-out, `\\DosDevices` namespace seeding, and object-manager
path traversal with suffix preservation, relative-target handling, and bounded
cycle/depth rejection. They remain distinct from VFS symlinks.
The graph now reaches `ntdll.dll!NtOpenThread`; its `CLIENT_ID` now resolves a
live NT scheduler task, verifies the owning process, and installs a typed
thread handle. Zero/out-of-range IDs, mismatched ownership, Linux targets, and
unknown access bits fail before publication. Thread query/termination and
handle lifetime remain owned by the existing canonical thread object path.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-02): named timer creation and opening
now reuse canonical scheduler-backed timer identity with collision,
type-mismatch, and missing-parent status handling; arming and cancellation
remain owned by the native monotonic timer primitive.
`NtPrivilegeCheck` is implemented against the canonical NT token privilege
owner, including enabled-privilege matching and all-required semantics.
The graph now reaches `ntdll.dll!NtPulseEvent`; native transient pulse
generation, wakeup, and manual/synchronization consumer semantics are now
implemented by the NT event owner.
`NtOpenKey` and `NtOpenKeyEx` now resolve typed key handles through the
canonical userspace registry owner; options outside the supported zero form
remain fail-closed. The Notepad handoff stages and starts that same owner
before launching the PE image; no kernel registry shadow database exists.
Windows NT frontier update (2026-09-02): named mutant creation and opening
now reuse canonical scheduler-backed mutant identity with collision,
type-mismatch, and missing-parent status handling; recursive ownership and
release remain owned by the native mutant primitive.
`NtQueryFullAttributesFile` now translates the Wine-shaped network-open
information record from canonical VFS metadata, including timestamps, size,
and regular-file or directory attributes.
`NtQueryInstallUILanguage` now returns the same en-US LANGID baseline as Wine's
system LCID fallback.
`NtQueryKey` is exposed at the NT boundary; registry key state remains owned by
the planned userspace registry service, as required by the Windows architecture
plan, rather than being duplicated inside the kernel.
`NtQueryPerformanceCounter` now shares the monotonic counter and 10 MHz
frequency owner used by the existing RTL performance-counter implementation.
`NtQuerySymbolicLinkObject` is exposed at the object-manager boundary; its
target data must come from the canonical NT symbolic-link namespace, not a
second interpretation of VFS symlinks.
`NtQuerySystemInformationEx` implements Wine's x86-64 supported-architecture
query with the native AMD64 record; other information classes remain explicit
until their owning kernel data source is available.
`NtQueryValueKey` is exposed for the userspace registry-service owner; the
kernel does not duplicate registry values or persistence state.
`NtQueryVolumeInformationFile` is exposed at the typed file boundary; its
implementation is pending a public VFS file-to-superblock accessor for the
canonical `statfs` owner.
`NtQueueApcThread` remains an explicit boundary until the scheduler owns a
Windows APC queue and x86-64 user APC return path; it must not be conflated
with Linux signal pending state.
`NtQueueApcThreadEx2` is exposed alongside the base APC boundary; reserve
handles, special APC flags, and callback delivery require that same owner.
`NtRaiseException` now copies and validates the caller's x86-64 context through
the canonical context decoder, retains it in the current thread's scheduler-
owned pending state, and enters the installed user dispatcher at return to
user mode. Breakpoint resumes apply the architectural instruction correction;
unsupported context components and non-x86-64 execution return an honest
unsupported status. Remote exception delivery remains outside this current-
thread owner.
`NtReadFileScatter` is exposed at the file boundary; segmented user-buffer
validation and completion require a scatter-read owner in the NT file adapter.
`NtReadVirtualMemory` current-process copying now uses the canonical
`elf-load::nt_memory` owner with destination-first validation, prefix byte
accounting, and `STATUS_PARTIAL_COPY` source-fault semantics. Cross-address-
space copying remains explicit until an address-space-aware owner exists.
`NtRemoveIoCompletionEx` now removes multiple packets through the canonical NT
completion-port queue; APC interruption remains dependent on the future APC
delivery owner.
`NtResetWriteWatch` now validates the current-process range before exposing the
honest `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` result; per-page dirty/write-protect ownership
remains a VMM task shared by both architectures.
`NtResumeThread` now uses a task-owned saturating NT suspend-depth transition
and encodes the prior depth; `NtSuspendThread` now increments that same owner,
while scheduler safe-point consumption remains to be wired.
`NtSaveKey` now validates the NT personality and non-null key/file handles
before returning `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`; a canonical registry-key object and
hive serializer over the VFS are still required.
`NtSetContextThread` now mutates the current x86_64 task's canonical syscall
frame for validated integer/control context fields; remote stopped-register
snapshots and non-x86 context owners remain unimplemented.
`NtSetInformationObject` now validates and stores the implemented
`ObjectHandleFlagInformation` class in the canonical handle table;
protect-from-close is enforced by every user-close path. Cross-process handle
inheritance and the remaining object-information classes remain explicit.
`NtSetInformationToken` now owns TokenSessionId mutation in the canonical
token object; default-DACL serialization and integrity-level SID ownership
remain explicit gaps.
`NtSetInformationVirtualMemory` now exposes the prefetch class boundary with
argument validation; VMM prefetch and per-page write-watch ownership remain
unimplemented.
`RtlCreateTimer` and `RtlCreateTimerQueue` validate output and callback
boundaries; callback dispatch, cancellation, and queue lifetime remain
unimplemented.
`RtlCreateUserStack` validates stack sizing and the INITIAL_TEB output boundary;
VMM allocation, guard-page policy, and NT-thread teardown remain unimplemented.
`RtlDeactivateActivationContext` and `RtlReleaseActivationContext` validate
their opaque handles/cookies; activation-frame and reference-count ownership
remain unimplemented.
`RtlDeleteAce` validates and compacts variable-sized caller-owned ACL entries;
concurrent ACL mutation remains outside the current native ownership model.
`RtlDeleteBarrier` preserves the reference no-op destruction boundary; barrier
waiter state and cancellation ownership remain unimplemented.
`RtlDeleteSecurityObject` releases descriptors through the canonical NT heap
owner; invalid foreign allocations are not yet reported with native fidelity.
`RtlDeleteTimer` and `RtlDeleteTimerQueueEx` validate queue/timer boundaries;
callback completion and timer-queue ownership remain unimplemented.
`RtlDeregisterWaitEx` removes the canonical wait record; completion-event
signaling remains unimplemented when a non-null event is supplied.
`RtlDeriveCapabilitySidsFromName` derives the reference UTF-16/SHA-256 SID
layouts for ASCII case folding; full Unicode uppercase mapping remains absent.
`RtlDestroyEnvironment` releases the environment block through the canonical
NT heap owner; invalid foreign allocations are not yet reported natively.
`RtlDestroyProcessParameters` releases the process-parameter block through the
canonical NT heap owner; parameter-record validation remains absent.
`RtlDoesFileExists_U` performs bounded UTF-16 conversion and queries the
canonical VFS resolver; full Windows path-device translation remains absent.
`RtlDosSearchPath_U` searches semicolon-separated paths through the canonical
VFS resolver and writes bounded UTF-16 output; full DOS search/current-directory
semantics remain absent.
`RtlDowncaseUnicodeChar` follows the reference ASCII fallback; full NLS
lowercase-table mapping is not yet connected.
`RtlCreateProcessParametersEx` validates its result-pointer boundary; native
string capture, environment ownership, normalization, and destruction remain
unimplemented.
`RtlCreateEnvironment` validates the native output-pointer boundary; inherited
environment cloning and NT environment-block allocation/lifetime remain
unimplemented.
`RtlCopySid` validates the native SID header and destination capacity, then
copies the bounded SID through the focused SID owner.
`RtlCreateActivationContext` validates the native `ACTCTXW` header and flag
mask; manifest parsing and activation-context object ownership remain absent.
`NtSetSystemInformation` accepts the Wine-compatible time-adjustment shape as
an explicit no-op; host clock ownership and privilege enforcement remain in
the kernel timekeeper/security layers.
`NtSetSystemTime` validates the NT personality and input pointers before an
explicit unsupported result; realtime offset ownership and privilege mapping
still need to be connected to the canonical timekeeper.
`NtSetValueKey` now exposes and validates the native six-argument ABI without
confusing it with the internal registry request record; canonical key/value
storage and VFS persistence remain unimplemented.
`NtUnloadKey` now validates its native `OBJECT_ATTRIBUTES` pointer boundary;
registry hive namespace and persistence ownership remain unimplemented.
`NtUnlockVirtualMemory` now rounds the current-process range and releases the
canonical VMA/PMM lock state; remote-process APC delivery remains unimplemented.
`NtUnmapViewOfSectionEx` supports the documented zero-flags current-process
path through the canonical VMA unmapper; extended flags and remote processes
remain unimplemented.
`NtWriteFileGather` now validates native length and I/O-status ordering, direct
regular-file options, page-aligned segment entries, and bounded segmented
source reads before writing through the canonical VFS file object. Short writes,
user-buffer faults, and zero-byte disk-full results preserve the completed byte
prefix in the NT I/O status and completion packet.
`NtWriteVirtualMemory` routes current-process writes through the canonical
`elf-load::nt_memory` owner, probes the readable source and writable target in
Wine's order, and reports the completed prefix as `STATUS_PARTIAL_COPY`;
cross-address-space copying remains unsupported until an address-space-aware
owner exists.
`NtYieldExecution` now routes through the canonical scheduler-yield owner for
the NT personality and compares the task's canonical voluntary/involuntary
context-switch counters, returning `STATUS_NO_YIELD_PERFORMED` when the
Linux-shaped scheduler immediately selects the caller again. Focused policy
tests include a positive control; x86_64 and aarch64 target checks pass.
`RtlActivateActivationContext` validates the native flags, opaque context, and
cookie-pointer boundary; per-thread activation-context stack and cookie
ownership remain unimplemented.
`RtlActivateActivationContextEx` validates the native TEB, opaque activation
context, and cookie-pointer boundary; TEB stack linking and cookie ownership
remain unimplemented.
`RtlAddAccessAllowedObjectAce` is cataloged with the reference implementation's
explicit unsupported result; canonical ACL mutation remains unimplemented.
`RtlAddAccessDeniedObjectAce` is cataloged with the reference implementation's
explicit unsupported result; canonical ACL mutation remains unimplemented.
`RtlAddAuditAccessObjectAce` is cataloged with the reference implementation's
explicit unsupported result; canonical ACL mutation remains unimplemented.
`RtlAddMandatoryAce` validates the mandatory-label ACE type and flag mask at
the NT security owner; self-relative ACL insertion remains unimplemented.
`RtlAddRefActivationContext` is cataloged as a void native boundary; the
activation-context object and its reference-count owner remain in userspace.
`RtlAllocateAndInitializeSid` allocates through the canonical NT heap owner and
writes the native SID layout; SID validation/free-lifetime edge cases remain.
`RtlAreAllAccessesGranted` implements the reference bitmask predicate in the
RTL owner; generic-access mapping remains the caller's responsibility.
`RtlAreAnyAccessesGranted` implements the companion reference bitmask
predicate; generic-access mapping remains the caller's responsibility.
`RtlBarrier` remains an explicit unsupported userspace synchronization boundary;
wait-on-address ownership and safe caller-memory barrier state are not present.
`RtlClearBits` mutates the caller-owned RTL bitmap with reference-compatible
range no-op behavior; atomicity across concurrent bitmap users remains absent.
`RtlCompactHeap` preserves the reference's harmless zero-byte compaction stub;
the canonical NT heap has no compaction operation to expose.
`RtlCompareUnicodeStrings` compares bounded UTF-16 code units with the
reference ASCII fallback for case-insensitive calls; full NLS case mapping is
not yet connected.
`RtlConvertToAutoInheritSecurityObject` preserves the reference implementation's
explicit `STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` result; auto-inheritance descriptor ownership
remains unimplemented.
`RtlCopyContext` implements the reference AMD64 flag validation and selected
legacy-context copy ranges; extended XSTATE and i386 context support remain
unimplemented.
`RtlEqualPrefixSid` validates native SID layouts and compares the prefix through
the final shared subauthority, preserving the reference count requirement.
`RtlEqualSid` validates native SID layouts and compares the complete SID byte
representation, including every subauthority.

| KI-0426 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | Desktop bridge handshake budget is barely larger than measured compositor startup | Measured 2026-09-06 in the guest: the compositor reports connect stage xcb-connected at 2775ms, atoms-interned 3166ms, xkb-extension 3185ms, keymap-ready 3407ms, display connected 3434ms, monitor geometry published 3448ms. The kernel bind handshake allows 5000ms, so a successful startup consumes 69 percent of the budget and any additional guest load pushes it over. Two runs failed with STATUS_IO_TIMEOUT while the child was still running and had produced no connected line. The X connection itself is the dominant cost at 2.8s. Widening the deadline alone would leave the kernel bounded-waiting on userspace service startup; the structural repair is for the launcher, not the kernel, to wait for its own child to become ready before binding. | Chris Watkins |
| KI-0428 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | Desktop background stays solid because gnome-shell image load fails silently in an unawaited async chain | Investigated 2026-09-06. Packaging is correct and was verified in the artifact, not the source: /usr/share/backgrounds/oxide/oxide-wallpaper.png is present 0644 with world-traversable parents in images/output/gnome-x86_64-root.img; 90-oxide-background.gschema.override is the only override touching org.gnome.desktop.background and compiles to the right default when queried with an isolated dconf; /etc/dconf/profile/user names local.d site.d distro.d and all three are empty; the oxide user has no ~/.config/dconf; and gdk-pixbuf-thumbnailer decoded the PNG successfully INSIDE the composed chroot, so the image is loadable and PNG support is compiled into libgdk_pixbuf. Root cause read out of the shipped gnome-shell code extracted from libshell-16.so in the composed image: Background._load calls _loadPattern first, which paints the solid primary-color, then calls _loadFile fire-and-forget without awaiting. _loadFile guards only the query_info_async content-type probe, and BackgroundCache.monitorFile calls file.monitor with no exception handling at all. Any throw in the async GIO stat or the inotify watch becomes an unhandled promise rejection that GJS logs and drops, leaving the black pattern drawn. Next step is to capture the GJS exception during a live boot with G_MESSAGES_DEBUG=all to name whether the failing syscall is the async statx/fstatat or inotify_init1/inotify_add_watch, then fix that syscall. Disabling the watch or the probe would be a workaround masking a real async-IO gap. | Chris Watkins |

| KI-0452 | OPEN | MISSING | med | PAGE_GUARD has no representation, so no fault can raise STATUS_GUARD_PAGE_VIOLATION | The reference resolves a guard-page fault inside its handler: it clears the guard bit, makes the page accessible and raises 0x80000001, or grows the thread stack. Oxide resolves faults in the address-space owner first and only refused faults reach sched::nt_exception::fault, which always answers STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION (deliberate, so no second copy of the resolution policy exists). Consequence: NT allocations carry no guard attribute, PAGE_GUARD in NtAllocateVirtualMemory/NtProtectVirtualMemory cannot round-trip, and a Windows thread that relies on guard-page stack growth or on a one-shot guard read gets an access violation instead. Fix belongs in the NT memory owner plus a vmm resolution arm, not in the exception decode. | unowned |
| KI-0453 | OPEN | MISSING | low | The exception frame carries no XSTATE, so an AVX thread resumes without its upper register halves | nt_exception_frame::deliver (B3523) writes the legacy FXSAVE image and a CONTEXT_EX whose XState chunk describes nothing (offset 0, length 25), which is exactly what the reference writes for a thread with no extended state. A thread that used AVX or AVX-512 and whose handler returns EXCEPTION_CONTINUE_EXECUTION resumes with only the legacy 512 bytes restored. Needs the XSAVE area appended after the 0x5c0 frame at 64-byte alignment, CONTEXT_XSTATE advertised, and nt_context_image::decode to stop answering Unsupported for it (it rejects CONTEXT_XSTATE today). | unowned |

The reference resolves the destination window AND its hit-test code at retrieval with a window_from_point walk that descends children and reports the nonclient codes (HTCAPTION, HTSYSMENU, the eight border codes, HTTRANSPARENT), and it converts screen coordinates to client coordinates there. oxide still resolves the target window and the client coordinates at post time (`ipc/src/win32_window/state.rs::post_pointer_with`, `compositor_pointer.rs::post_compositor_pointer`), so the nonclient renumbering the ladder implements (WM_NCMOUSEMOVE range, wParam = hit code) can never fire and a click over a frame region is reported as a client click. Closing this means moving target resolution and hit-testing into the retrieval stage and posting raw screen-coordinate input. | chris@watkinslabs.com |

Measured against a real GNOME XWayland (DISPLAY=:0, mutter 48.8, host output idle): xdotool getdisplaygeometry reports "0 0" and xrandr "current 0 x 0" while _NET_WORKAREA on the same root reads 0,0,1920,1032, and the bridge exits at startup. The root selects only PROPERTY_CHANGE, so no ConfigureNotify/RRScreenChangeNotify is handled either: an XWayland whose outputs appear after the bridge connects, or a guest resolution change, leaves GetSystemMetrics and the work area permanently wrong with no path to correct them. | chris@watkinslabs.com |

What still cannot fail hosted is the ordinal-level claim: the ~60 family routers expose no ungated claims(ordinal) predicate -- their ordinal sets live inside gated kernel::route match arms -- so nothing asserts that no two routers claim one ordinal, nor that every ordinal raw_args::argument_count admits is claimed by exactly one router. Today ordering silently resolves one known overlap (Family::KeyboardQuery precedes Family::Legacy, whose raw_ordinal_claimed set contains the four keyboard-state ordinals). Design: give each family an ungated claims(ordinal) -> bool in its already-ungated parent module (the ordinal constants are there), make the gated route delegate to it as its first statement so admission has one owner, then assert over the raw_args admitted universe that exactly one family claims each ordinal |  |

| KI-0664 | OPEN | PERF | med | Typing cadence in the Notepad acceptance run was paced by the harness, not by the guest input path: the guest retrieves a character every 11 ms when QEMU is not asked to queue it | Premise of the original row disproved by measurement. (1) Harness send rate: the QMP `send-key` command itself returns in 0.53 ms median (30 calls through tools/notepad_qmp.py against a live qemu-system-x86-core 9.2.4), so the harness is not slow at issuing keystrokes. (2) QEMU's own QMP reference documents `send-key`'s `hold-time` as defaulting to 100 ms over a serial input queue. (3) Decisive in-guest A/B, one acceptance run (/home/nd/oxide/tgt-B3567/acc/cadence-4165093.md, uart-4165093.log, fixture tools/tests/fixtures/cadence-b3567-phases.log): the same eight characters typed into the same control three ways, WM_CHAR-to-WM_CHAR medians from the guest's own trace -- `send-key` at the default hold 76.0 ms, `send-key` at a 5 ms hold 74.0 ms, `input-send-event` (not queued) 11.0 ms. The hold is not the pacer, the queue behind `send-key` is; a guest costing 80 ms per character could not produce the 11 ms row. The earlier 79-81 ms medians (tgt-B3562, tgt-B3566) were the harness's own delivery, not the guest's. Fixed on the harness side at 95ee98c2f/this branch: text now leaves through `input-send-event`, chords keep an explicit 5 ms hold, and every run prints a per-phase cadence table. Remaining real work under this row: the guest's own 11 ms per character (8-12 ms with one 62 ms outlier) and the 46 ms token phase measured while text was still on `send-key`; both are guest-clock timestamps, so they compare against each other rather than wall clock. | chris@watkinslabs.com |
| KI-0666 | OPEN | PERF | med | A first whole-window frame spends up to 287 ms in the socket write because the payload is many times the socket cap | Startup frames in the notepad acceptance run carry the whole surface (about 1.5 MB) while nt_compositor SOCKET_CAP is 256 KB, so stream::write_record blocks in write_chunk/arm_stream_write for one consumer drain per chunk. Measured at 4b8fe53d0 (tgt-B3566/acc/uart-4017595.log): write_us 0.01 / 31.16 / 144.03 / 287.53 ms for the four frames over 8 KB, with pickup 74.58 / 79.56 / 87.72 / 180.68 ms as the frames behind them wait for socket capacity. Typing frames (1.4 KB and under) are unaffected: write 0-2 us. This is genuine back pressure rather than the head-of-line rule KI-0659 removed, but a first paint still costs a quarter of a second on the wire. | chris@watkinslabs.com |
| KI-0667 | OPEN | INFRA | low | Two harness pytest cases are red on main: the QMP serial-wait concurrency case and the teardown body-order case | On a clean origin/main tree (/home/nd/oxide/kernel, e57ba6512) `python3 -m pytest -q tools/perf/` reports 3 failed, 77 passed. One is the already-filed KI-0594. The other two are new rows here: tools/perf/test_notepad_qmp.py::QmpTests::test_other_client_completes_during_real_serial_wait fails at `self.assertTrue(waiting.wait(1))` — main() never reaches the patched launch_on_desktop within a second, so the other-client QMP session is never proven concurrent; and tools/perf/test_windows_teardown_wiring.py::TeardownStructuralTests::test_production_cleanup_body_order fails on the order of the production cleanup body. Both reproduce identically on B3567-input-cadence-and-bridge-poll, which touches neither path (its own additions are tools/perf/test_notepad_typing_rate.py and tools/tests/test_notepad_cadence.py, both green). | chris@watkinslabs.com |
| KI-0668 | OPEN | INFRA | high | Guest Wine runtime is copied from the build host instead of built and packaged: staging is not reproducible and the DLLs need not match the Wine source the kernel is developed against. | tools/xtask/src/rootfs_disks/windows_notepad.rs:107 wine_root() reads OXIDE_WINE_RUNTIME_ROOT defaulting to /usr/lib64/wine; line 112 requires OXIDE_WINE_NTDLL and OXIDE_WINE_WIN32U; line 46 OXIDE_WINE_NLS defaults to /usr/share/wine/nls/locale.nls. It is the only host-source reader under rootfs_disks/ (grep for env::var_os there returns one other, an unrelated syslog flag); every other injector builds its probe from repo source. /home/nd/oxide/images/build.sh and all four files under images/profiles/ contain zero occurrences of wine, so the image compose never installs it: the guest gets whatever Wine the build machine has (measured: wine-core 10.20-3.fc42) while the master reference tree is Wine 11.16. Fix is a packaged oxide-wine built by packagectl into packages/repo/<arch>, listed in the image profile, with the host paths and env overrides removed. | a7b0bd29c42f59f8c |
| KI-0669 | OPEN | MISSING | high | Creation and destruction never send WM_PARENTNOTIFY, so a child control identifier is never reported to its parent | Reference sends WM_PARENTNOTIFY to the parent of an effective child without WS_EX_NOPARENTNOTIFY at both ends of the window lifetime, wparam MAKEWPARAM(WM_CREATE\|WM_DESTROY, GWLP_ID) and lparam the child HWND, sent synchronously after the create geometry pass and before the destroy hide. This tree sends WM_PARENTNOTIFY only from the pointer ladder (crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_window/hardware/context.rs parent_notify, idChild 0, which matches the reference for mouse). Grep of crates/kernel for WM_PARENTNOTIFY finds no create or destroy site. Consequence: a parent dialog never learns a control was created or destroyed, and the identifier stored by B3568 has no lifetime consumer. Create side needs a callback phase in nt_window/create.rs complete_callback CALLBACK_CREATE, destroy side in the destroy order walk. Found auditing KI-0391. | unowned |
| KI-0670 | OPEN | COVERAGE | high | nt_wine_window inner test modules are phantoms; only the declared [[test]] harnesses ever compile | crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_wine_window is kernel-gated, so every #[cfg(test)] mod inside it compiles out of cargo test and the count never moves. Measured: crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_wine_window/tests/create_abi.rs called raw_arg(), a function that exists nowhere in the tree, and cargo test -p syscalls stayed green. The hosted [[test]] binary nt-wine-create-window (src/nt_wine_window/tests/create_window_hosted.rs) had also stopped compiling on origin/main: it called raw_class::create_window, removed when the entry became create_window_descriptor, and its mod nt_window lacked register_class_desc_for_current; 57 tests including the child-control-id assertions had not run. Repaired in B3568. Remaining work: audit every other #[cfg(test)] mod under nt_wine_window and nt_window for the same phantom class, and add a gate that fails when a declared [[test]] path does not build. | unowned |
| KI-0671 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | Window class atoms come from a per-process counter beside the existing global user atom table: two independent atom spaces that can collide | ipc::win32_window mints class atoms from WindowManager::next_atom, a per-process counter starting at 1, while the tree already has ipc::win32_window::UserAtomTable (global USER_ATOMS, starting at 0xC001) used by RegisterWindowMessage, window properties and NtUserGetAtomName. The reference takes the class atom from that same table. Consequences: NtUserGetAtomName cannot resolve a class atom, and the two spaces can hand out the same number for different things. This is a split source of truth. Not fixed in B3570 because it changes the atom-allocation signature across the create paths and the builtin class registrations; it wants its own lane. Found while closing KI-0524 and KI-0625. | unowned |
| KI-0672 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | Menu lookup by command identifier never descends into submenus | The reference find_menu_item recurses into every popup and falls back to a matching popup item, so a by-command call against a menu-bar handle finds items in its dropdowns. MenuManager::item, position and item_mut in crates/kernel/ipc/src/win32_menu do not recurse, so every by-command call against a menu-bar handle misses submenu items entirely: enable, check, and item-info calls addressed to the bar silently do nothing for the items a real application actually owns. Found while closing KI-0632. | unowned |
| KI-0673 | OPEN | DEFECT | low | Two more UTF-16 to ANSI converters remain in kernel-gated files beside the single owner | crates/kernel/ipc/src/win32_text.rs::utf16_to_ansi is now the one owner (nt_rtl_ansi::convert_utf16 was repointed at it, so the menu path and the RTL path share one implementation and one test suite). Two further encoders still live in kernel-gated files, nt_path.rs and nt_unicode.rs, where their behaviour cannot be hosted-tested and can drift from the owner. | KI-0674 | OPEN | COVERAGE | med | No boot had confirmed that a power-button request actually reaches the guest; aarch64 is still unmeasured | The acceptance harness and boot-smoke both ended a run by killing QEMU, so nothing had ever exercised an orderly guest shutdown. Now answered on x86_64 by this branch: the guest reaches basic.target in 13s and then IGNORES the power button, killed at the 45s grace, with zero shutdown lines in 309 kernel lines of serial output. aarch64 has not been measured at all. The three-way outcome (powered-off, ignored, began-but-unfinished) is now reported by boot-smoke as shutdown=..., so the question is answerable from any run rather than needing a bespoke boot. | unowned |
| KI-0675 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | ACPI fixed events are registered but their enable bit is never set, so the power button interrupt is dropped | crates/kernel/firmware/src/acpi/events.rs register_fixed_event only records a handler. init() then zeroes the whole PM1 enable half via disable_fixed_events and never sets the bit back for an owned event, while fixed_event_irq gates delivery on status AND enable. So PWRBTN_STS arrives with PWRBTN_EN clear and is discarded: the guest cannot see a power button press at all. Measured on x86_64: QMP system_powerdown produces no shutdown activity and the guest runs on until the harness kills it. Read out of the code rather than inferred from the symptom. Fix site named; out of lane for B3572. | unowned |
| KI-0676 | OPEN | COVERAGE | high | Windows call shape identity is verified mechanically but shape content is not: no check compares a structure we fill against what the DLL expects | Three layers verify the Windows surface today and they cover progressively less. Ordinal numbers are DECODED out of the shipped win32u.dll (stub_ordinal reads the stub bytes and takes the immediate), so a Wine version bump cannot silently renumber the syscalls under us; that is strong. make windows-surface-gate walks every import descriptor, delay descriptor and forwarder in the real PE closure and fails on an unresolvable name or an unadmitted ordinal; that is strong and is what caught the 47 unbindable imports before a boot. Argument counts come from our own table keyed by the decoded ordinal; medium, the count is ours even though the ordinal is not. Struct layouts, field offsets and NTSTATUS values rest only on hosted tests written by hand from the reference plus the boot-time notepad-uart-audit; that is the gap. Nothing compares a structure we fill in against what the DLL actually expects byte for byte. A wrong field offset in a structure user32 passes to a window syscall leaves the surface gate green and the ordinal correct, and shows up as a garbled window or a crash at boot. The uart audit only catches a call that was REFUSED, never one that was silently mis-served. Now buildable rather than aspirational: the reference headers the DLLs were compiled against are pinned in vendor/wine, so a generated-offsets check can assert our layouts against them. | unowned |
| KI-0683 | OPEN | PERF | med | Every retrieved pointer message now costs a window-procedure round trip for its hit test | B3564 (refiled after rebase). The retrieval-time hardware stage sends WM_NCHITTEST for every pointer message no capture answers, which is what the reference does, but in oxide a send to the application is a client callback that unwinds and resumes the syscall, so a stream of mouse motion costs one suspend/resume per message where the reference pays a cheap in-process call. Measure against the pump budget before assuming it is affordable; if it is not, the fix is a kernel-side answer for windows whose procedure is the default one, never a cached code that can disagree with what the window would answer. | chris@watkinslabs.com |
| KI-0684 | OPEN | INFRA | med | Twenty-three NT GUI call sites still open-code the process entry lookup, each reserving a whole GuiEntry on its own stack | B3564 (refiled after rebase). Each is the same three lines and each pays the same frame cost; they are also one lookup rule split across twelve files. | chris@watkinslabs.com |

| KI-0690 | OPEN | INFRA | med | tools/next-branch.sh --claim cannot claim a number while any sibling worktree is stale | The claim ref is pushed with git push, which runs .githooks/pre-push, which runs tools/worktree-guard.sh and rejects when any linked worktree is older than 2 hours. On 2026-09-07 /home/nd/oxide/kernel-B3564 (detached, age 7324s, another lane) blocked every claim: next-branch.sh retried 25 numbers and reported FAILED to claim a B number in 25 attempts, which reads like a counter exhaustion rather than a hook rejection. The guard exists to police code publication; a claim ref carries no code and one lane cannot reconcile another lane worktree, so a stale sibling silently denies every new lane its counter. | unowned |

| KI-0697 | OPEN | MISSING | med | SendInput drops the dwExtraInfo of every injected record, so a retrieval can never report hardware extra information | crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_wine_window/input_raw/kernel.rs send_input reads only dx/dy/mouseData/dwFlags (offsets 8,12,16,20) and wVk/dwFlags (8,12); the INPUT record carries dwExtraInfo at offset 32 (mouse) and 24 (keyboard) and neither is read. The value would have to reach the queued message so a retrieval records it; the injected transitions go through apply_step into the backend input path, which carries no extra-information field. Consequence: NtUserGetMessageExtraInfo and the ExtraInfo thread-state class report only what NtUserSetMessageExtraInfo (win32u 0x156d, admitted in B3574) stored, and a retrieval resets it to zero. Reference behaviour: a retrieval records the hardware extra information of the message it read, zero for a posted message. | unassigned |

| KI-0714 | OPEN | INFRA | high | Sixteen hosted test targets fail on main and a workspace run cannot complete, so no lane can report a clean hosted suite | Measured on a branch whose changes touch none of the failing crates. A full cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast run completed only after killing a wedged fs test binary: 885 targets green, 31321 tests passed, 16 targets FAILING. The failing targets are block, fs (x4), net, overlayfs, power-supply, security, slab, sysfs, and syscalls (x5). FIVE of the sixteen were verified to fail IDENTICALLY on a clean origin/main checkout, same test names and same pass/fail counts: slab --lib (3 failures), security --test selinux_scheduler, fs --test devfs_xattr_store, sysfs --lib, overlayfs --lib. The other ELEVEN are NOT verified against main and this row must not be read as claiming they are; five were checked, not sixteen. Separately and compounding it, cargo test -p fs shows no progress for ~13 minutes at 1305/1401 with 45 tests reported running past 60s, all under crates/kernel/fs/src/perf (perf::inherit 17, perf::throttle 11, perf::switch 11, perf::registry 4, perf::hrtimer 2), the binary sitting in futex wait with 46 threads; reproduced on a clean origin/main with its own target and temp directories. That was observed as a stall over a window, NOT proven non-termination, since both runs were killed by hand; a slow suite and a deadlock need different first moves. Consequence: nothing ordered after fs in a workspace run is exercised, so every lane in this campaign reported per-crate counts rather than a workspace total. Next step is to verify the remaining eleven against clean main and split this into per-crate rows. | unowned |

| KI-0722 | OPEN | INFRA | low | Three NT files already past the 1000-line error cap grew further when the 11.16 runtime routines were published | crates/kernel/exec/src/pe_loader.rs 1474 to 1520, crates/kernel/syscall/src/nt.rs 1615 to 1664, crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_dispatch.rs 1757 to 1769. | KI-0723 | OPEN | MISSING | med | The slim reader/writer lock wakes every sleeper on a release because both waiter classes share one futex key | The Windows lock parks a blocked writer on the owner half of the lock word, two bytes above its base, and a blocked reader on the whole word, so a release can disturb one class without the other. A futex key covers a whole aligned word, and the address-wait service every other user of this word goes through keys on that same word, so both classes land in one key here. Waking a single sleeper on a shared key can leave the intended one parked, so crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_srw/dispatch.rs wakes all on every release: a superset of the reference wakes, never a subset. crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_srw/state.rs owns wait_address and futex_key and carries the reference decision (Wake::OneWriter against Wake::AllOnWord), so a finer wake channel consumes them unchanged; a test pins that the two addresses differ and that the key is wider than the offset between them. Cost is spurious wakeups under contention, not incorrectness. | chris@watkinslabs.com |
| KI-0724 | OPEN | MISSING | med | A dynamic unwind registration that supplies entries through a callback cannot be consulted, because entering the callback is user work the entry lookup runs as a kernel service | RtlInstallFunctionTableCallback registrations are admitted and stored (crates/kernel/sched/src/nt_function_table.rs) and crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_function_table.rs::lookup finds them, but returns no entry for one: the producer callback is user code and the lookup runs as a kernel entry reached from RtlLookupFunctionEntry, which has no way to call out to it. Static registrations from RtlAddFunctionTable resolve fully, including chained entries. Closing this needs the entry lookup to run in user mode, which is the module split KI-0705 describes. | chris@watkinslabs.com |

| KI-0727 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | RtlAddFunctionTable answered an allocation failure with a status, which a boolean-returning export reads as success | The three dynamic unwind registration exports publish BOOLEAN, not NTSTATUS. crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_function_table.rs::add returned STATUS_NO_MEMORY (0xc000_0017) when the registration list could not be reserved; that value is nonzero, so a caller testing the boolean read a failed registration as success and would go on to rely on an entry table the process never recorded. Found by make feature-gate, whose unused-constant error on the leftover STATUS_SUCCESS was the tell that these functions had been reshaped from status-returning to boolean-returning without the failure path following. The shim carries #![cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")] so no test block in it ever compiled. Fixed by moving the answer to ungated crates/kernel/sched/src/nt_function_table.rs (registration_answer, record, retire), which every registration service now answers through; tests assert every answer is a boolean and that a status in that position is nonzero. Positive control: failure path answering 0xc000_0017 turns two tests red, restoring turns them green. install_callback and delete were checked for the same class and were already correct. | chris@watkinslabs.com |

| KI-0742 | OPEN | DEFECT | blocker | Every service reached through the raw system-service ordinal route received the wrong arguments, because a stock service stub moves only the first one | A stock stub in the staged runtime module is exactly: move the first argument into the register the syscall instruction leaves free, load the ordinal, test the shared-page dispatch byte, syscall, return (disassembled at RVA 0x7cb48 and 0x7ca28 of target/artifacts/wine/x86_64/x86_64-windows/ntdll.dll). It performs no other marshalling, so arguments two to four stay in the caller-convention registers and arguments five and six stay in the caller frame at +0x28 and +0x30. The synthetic runtime page this route replaced DID marshal all six (pe::nt_stub::encode_x64_six_arg_stub), so every handler decode is written against argument positions and every one of them read a scratch register as its first argument. Measured effect: the process-heap reservation the runtime issues first was refused, RtlCreateHeap returned zero, peb->ProcessHeap stayed null and the next allocation faulted at ntdll RVA 0x33fbf, 0xdf into RtlAllocateHeap, dereferencing offset 0x8a0 (struct heap bins) of a null heap; the size argument at the fault, 0x500, is exactly the 32-entry TLS directory array allocated on the statement after the heap is created, which places the fault after heap creation rather than before it. Fixed on B3586 by converting the entry record once at the raw ordinal boundary (syscall::nt::windows_abi::windows_args, called from syscalls/src/dispatch/core.rs), which reproduces exactly the record the synthetic stubs produced, so no handler changes. Cost: two user-stack reads per raw-ordinal entry regardless of the service arity. | B3586 |
| KI-0743 | OPEN | DEFECT | blocker | Native virtual allocations were placed on page granularity, so the runtime heap recovered region bases that named unmapped memory | The reference places every view and private allocation the kernel chooses on the system allocation granularity, 0x10000 (map_view defaults its alignment mask to the granularity mask; a reserving request with a supplied base rounds that base DOWN to the granularity while a pure commit rounds to a page). Oxide placed them on the 0x1000 page granularity. The heap recovers a region base by rounding an interior address down to the granularity, so an allocation whose base was page-aligned but not granularity-aligned yielded a recovered base below the allocation. Measured on boot log target/boot-logs/x86_64-20260908-001706.log: [WINDOWS-PE-FAULT] va=00007f56f9460018 rip=000000017003a483 rcx=00007f56f9460000 rdx=r8=00007f56f946d038 r9=00000000000fffc0 result=invalid vma=none. Fault RVA 0x3a483 is +0x13 into the 0x19-byte function at 0x3a470 whose .pdata entry covers it; it computes base + 0x40 + *(base + 0x18), which is subheap_commit_end over SUBHEAP (sizeof 0x40, data_size at 0x18). r9 = 0xfffc0 is a 0x100000 grow-size subheap less its header, so the region had just been created. The block at +0xd038 was mapped and the recovered base 0x10000 below it was not. Fixed on B3586: vmm gained MmapPlacement::AdvisoryAligned so a caller can require a coarser granule than the backing does (the hole search already took an alignment for huge pages), elf_load::nt_memory places every kernel-chosen NT region on ALLOCATION_GRANULARITY and rounds a reserving base down to it, and the two NT section-view placements that let the kernel choose were moved to the same granule. | B3586 |
| KI-0746 | OPEN | INFRA | med | The synthetic 562-stub NT runtime page is reachable only when the catalog stages no runtime module, which the shipped launcher always does | crates/kernel/exec/src/pe_loader.rs::map_nt_runtime is now called from exactly one production site, syscalls/src/pe_exec.rs::prepare_pe_process, on the branch taken when catalog.load(RUNTIME_MODULE) returns None. B3588 made the launcher hand the kernel the runtime module it loads from, and B3587 stages ntdll.dll into the image, so that branch is not taken in the shipped configuration. The page carries a 562-entry handwritten export catalog (ntdll_catalog.rs), a per-index service selector table and its stub encoders, all kept alive by tests only. Same shape as the support-page orphan fixed by KI-0745: correct code with no live caller. Measured while wiring the support region on B3589; the support region itself was extracted out of this page and is now shared by both paths, so removing the stub half is a self-contained follow-up. | unassigned |
| KI-0747 | OPEN | COVERAGE | med | Two runtime-handover tests and the Windows call-surface gate report green when the Wine catalog is absent, so a green from them proves nothing about the catalog path | Measured on B3589: with target/artifacts moved aside, cargo test -p elf-load pe_runtime_loader reports "5 passed" in 0.00s and cargo test -p syscalls --test windows_call_surface reports "82 passed" in 0.00s - the same lines a real run prints. elf-load/src/pe_runtime_loader/tests.rs returns early from staged() when the fixture is missing; the surface gate is KI-0739.
