| KI-0748 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | A mapped PE image reports MEM_MAPPED or MEM_PRIVATE from NtQueryVirtualMemory, never MEM_IMAGE | crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_dispatch.rs:1749 derives MEMORY_BASIC_INFORMATION.Type from NtMemoryInfo.mapped_view alone: 0x40000 MEM_MAPPED when the VMA carries VmaFlags::NT_SECTION_VIEW, else 0x20000 MEM_PRIVATE. Windows reports 0x1000000 MEM_IMAGE for any page of a view of an image section, and the reference sets it from the SEC_IMAGE bit on the view rather than from the mapping being a view at all. The kernel PE image loader (elf-load pe_loader::load_pe_image_with_resolver_at_mode) sets neither NT_SECTION_VIEW nor any image marker, so every mapped module reports MEM_PRIVATE. Found while fixing the AllocationBase half of the same structure on B3589; not the current blocker, because the runtime module PE side reads AllocationBase and never reads Type - grep for MEM_IMAGE across the reference PE-side sources returns nothing. Closing it needs an image marker on the VMA, which the VMM does not yet carry. | unassigned |
| KI-0751 | IN-PROGRESS B3589-nt-runtime-support-page | DEFECT | blocker | [CLAIMED B3589-nt-runtime-support-page 2026-09-07] The runtime still exits with STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND loading kernel32.dll, cause not yet established | Boot log target/boot-logs/x86_64-20260908-012822.log: "wine: could not load kernel32.dll, status c0000135" then process exit 53, which is that status truncated to its low byte by the reference unix exit-code conversion (a nonzero status whose low byte is zero becomes 1, otherwise the status is the exit code). Measured and DISPROVED on B3589: the module is staged and reachable - target/builds/notepad-1567407/root-x86_64.img has /windows/c/windows/system32 as a symlink to /usr/local/lib/oxide/windows/x86_64-windows, which contains kernel32.dll; the path translation is right - nt_path::normalize_path maps C:\\windows\\system32\\kernel32.dll to /windows/c/windows/system32/kernel32.dll; the search list is not empty - the reference builds it from a fixed system path even when ProcessParameters DllPath is null, so the system directory is always probed; the access mask is not the refusal - the open uses GENERIC_READ\|SYNCHRONIZE, which passed the check even before it was fixed; and the current directory is not consulted, since a drive-absolute DOS path is converted without it. The reference open_dll_file reports DLL_NOT_FOUND whenever its open fails AND a following attribute query also fails, so the real status is masked at the point of the message. Remaining suspects, in order: the attribute query path, symlink traversal in the Windows lookup, and the NtCreateSection SEC_IMAGE step. The next boot names it directly: nt_file.rs now reports every failing NT path open as [WINDOWS-NT-OPEN-FAIL] status=.. path=.., bounded to 64 and excluding a merely absent name. | unassigned |
| KI-0752 | OPEN | MISSING | med | The NT process-parameter block never writes DllPath, and the runtime handover discards the process parameters its caller supplies | exec/src/process_env/builder.rs writes CurrentDirectory at 0x38, its handle at 0x48, ImagePathName at 0x60, CommandLine at 0x70, WindowTitle at 0xb0 and the environment at 0x80, but nothing at 0x50, so RTL_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS.DllPath stays a zeroed UNICODE_STRING. The reference tolerates a null DllPath by falling back to a load path built from the image path plus a fixed system path, so this is not the current blocker, but a non-null empty DllPath would suppress that fallback and leave the search list empty - the field must be written correctly or not at all. Separately, exec/src/pe_runtime_loader.rs::load builds its parameters from NtProcessParameters::default_for() and ignores the params argument that syscalls/src/pe_exec.rs::prepare_pe_process is given, so an inherited current directory, console handle and standard handle triplet are dropped on the handover path while the synthetic path honours them. Measured on B3589 while diagnosing the kernel32 exit. | unassigned |
| KI-0755 | OPEN | DEFECT | critical | Every NT object name is undecodable: the counted-name length is read 32 bits wide, folding the buffer capacity into the count | Measured on the acceptance boot of main d0ab229e0 (target/windows-notepad-acceptance/uart-1933653.log): with the shipped runtime module driving its own loader, [WINDOWS-NT-OPEN-FAIL] status=00000000c000000d path=<undecodable-object-name> for the working directory, then "wine: could not load kernel32.dll, status c0000135" and no window. crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_file.rs::object_name read the UNICODE_STRING count with read_u32, which on this ABI returns Length \| MaximumLength << 16; a name-initialising routine sets MaximumLength = Length + 2, so the folded value always exceeds the length bound and every name was refused with STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER. The in-kernel PE graph loader opened modules through the VFS directly, so nothing exercised this decode until the runtime handover landed. Siblings read the count 16 bits wide (nt_registry.rs read_unicode, nt_directory.rs read_name), so this site was the outlier. | B3591-nt-object-name-length |
| KI-0756 | OPEN | DEFECT | critical | NtCreateSection refused the standard rights every module-section request carries, so no module could be mapped | Measured on the acceptance boot of main e32850298 (uart-1945033.log): with the name decode fixed, "wine: could not load kernel32.dll, status c000000d" with no [WINDOWS-NT-OPEN-FAIL] line, so the refusal came after the open. crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_dispatch.rs admitted only SECTION_QUERY\|SECTION_MAP_READ\|SECTION_MAP_WRITE\|SECTION_MAP_EXECUTE\|SYNCHRONIZE, while the loader asks STANDARD_RIGHTS_REQUIRED\|SECTION_QUERY\|SECTION_MAP_READ\|SECTION_MAP_EXECUTE. Every wait on more than one object waits on the wrong objects. Owned by the nt_dispatch lane; found by B3604 signature audit. | B3604 |
| KI-0845 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | NtGetWriteWatch judges its flags argument as a full 64-bit word | nt_dispatch.rs tests "call.args.a1 & !WRITE_WATCH_FLAG_RESET != 0" on the whole register. The flags argument is a ULONG, so only its low half is the value; a caller whose register carries anything above it is refused with STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER. Fix = crate::nt_obj_sig::ulong(a1). Owned by the nt_dispatch lane; found by B3604 signature audit. | B3604 |
| KI-0846 | OPEN | MISSING | med | No NT service taking more than six arguments can work on aarch64 | crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_dispatch.rs stack_argument() is x86-64 only and returns None on every other target, and SyscallArgs carries six words. The AArch64 procedure call standard passes eight arguments in registers, so NtSetTimer, NtDuplicateObject, NtCreateKey, NtAccessCheck, NtGetWriteWatch, NtNotifyChangeKey and NtCreateUserProcess all answer STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER on ARM instead of running. Needs the arm64 argument registers x6/x7 (and the frame beyond) plumbed into the NT entry record. | B3604 |
| KI-0847 | OPEN | MISSING | med | A timer armed with an APC callback and a named completion port are both refused for want of an owner | crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_timer.rs set_timer answers STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED when NtSetTimer carries a non-null APC routine (the reference queues the routine on expiry), and nt_completion.rs create_io_completion answers STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED for a NtCreateIoCompletion with object attributes, because completion ports are not published in the named-object namespace. Both are honest refusals filed with their fix, not silent successes. | B3604 |
| KI-0848 | OPEN | DEFECT | low | NtCreateKey always reports the key as newly created and refuses every non-zero option | crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_registry.rs create_key writes 1 (created) into the disposition output unconditionally, so a caller cannot tell an opened key from a created one, and refuses any non-zero options word (REG_OPTION_VOLATILE, CREATE_LINK, BACKUP_RESTORE) with STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER. The registry owner protocol carries no disposition or option today. | B3604 |
| KI-0849 | OPEN | MISSING | low | The registry change notification answers only the exact last-value-set filter | crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_registry_policy.rs supported_request requires filter == REG_NOTIFY_CHANGE_LAST_SET exactly, so a caller asking for name, attribute or security changes - alone or combined with LAST_SET, which is what a caller of RegNotifyChangeKeyValue usually asks - is answered STATUS_NOT_IMPLEMENTED. The refusal is deliberate while the owner reports value mutations only; the fix is the missing change classes in the registry owner protocol. | B3604 |
| KI-0781 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | NtQueryDefaultLocale is reached with its second argument holding 1, which is not an address any caller passes (KI-0773 half) | Measured, not theorised: the service takes (BOOLEAN user, LCID *lcid) and the runtime table maps its ordinal to exactly that service (the reference numbering gives that ordinal a two-argument, sixteen-byte entry, and the boot trace logs the same ordinal-to-service pair). The kernel half is correct - the selector is read as one byte, so a0=0x35000 is an ordinary FALSE whose upper bits are a stale register, and only the output pointer is judged. The refusal comes from a1=1, an argument no declared caller of this service passes and which cannot be dereferenced; the value is lost before the kernel sees it, i.e. on the delivery side (register/frame marshalling for this route), not in nt_locale. B3604 also changed the refusal status from STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER to STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION, which is what an unwritable output answers. Needs a traced boot of the caller to name the delivery defect. | B3604 |
| KI-0850 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | The PE image registry is never cleared when an address space is torn down | B3605: pe_modules::clear has no caller outside tests, and mm-pmm exit_mmap/mmdrop do not touch it, so every NT process leaves its image records keyed by a root_pa that is then recycled. No live misroute follows: the loader paths publish with register(), which replaces the whole list for a root, and only an NT-personality task consults the records. The leak is unbounded across process churn. | nt-loader |
| KI-0782 | OPEN | INFRA | low | Several ledger ids are used by more than one row on main | B3605: scratch/known_issues.md lines 1020 and 1026 both carry id KI-0778 (the NtMapViewOfSectionEx ULONG narrowing row and the raw-NT-ordinal misroute row). tools/issues.sh --show KI-0778 prints only the first, so --fix on that id is ambiguous. tools/issues.sh --check on a clean origin/main also reports KI-0776, KI-0777 and KI-0779 duplicated across eleven rows. Parallel lanes mint ids from their local ledger copy; renumbering at rebase was missed. KI-0778 was split in B3605 by renumbering the misroute row to KI-0783. | ledger |
| KI-0784 | IN-PROGRESS B3607-unixlib-init-contract | DEFECT | high | [CLAIMED B3607-unixlib-init-contract 2026-09-08] Wine runtime unix-call handle required a guest .so to publish a table; win32u.so 11.16 publishes none | The launcher bootstrap failed with "native registration failed: InvalidInput" because it dlsym(win32u.so, "__wine_unix_call_funcs") and got NULL: the shipped 11.16 win32u.so exports __wine_unix_lib_init only (nm -D shows one wine symbol, __wine_unix_lib_init), because 11.16 win32u registers a SYSTEM SERVICE TABLE at index 1 rather than a unix-call table. The kernel compounded this: the runtime handle (WINE_UNIXLIB_HANDLE) resolved to "the first table published in this address space" and then required lookup_unixlib_entry to succeed, so the kernel-served eight-function table could not be reached at all in a process with no published guest table -- even though the callable it validated was never entered (complete_x64_unix_call ignores it). Fixed on B3607: the runtime handle is served by the kernel table with no guest object; the launcher stops loading win32u.so; the launcher resolver now follows the reference (funcs, else init entry which it CALLS, else STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND). | chris |
| KI-0787 | OPEN | MISSING | med | win32u.so has no loadable Unix half on this kernel, so its unixlib query is refused | uart-2907716.log: [WINDOWS-NT-UNIXLIB] query=win32u.so -> load=einval -> NtQueryVirtualMemory(MemoryWineLoadUnixLib, a1=win32u PE base) status c000000d. Confirmed NOT the crash cause: the PE-side initializer stores the handle and its caller ignores the return value, leaving handle 0 and a non-null init dispatcher; win32u PE makes no unix call in this version, its NtUser*/NtGdi* entries being table-1 syscalls this kernel serves. The kernel is win32k, so there is no guest Unix half to load; the query should be answered from the kernel table rather than by loading an ELF, once anything actually calls through it. | chris |
| KI-0788 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | Compositor bridge held the requested window extent, not the one the display server reported, so every frame after a WM resize was refused | The backend kept Window.width/height from the outbound Create/Geometry records only; the inbound ConfigureNotify path (poll_event Configure arm) forwarded the new rectangle to the kernel without adopting it locally. The kernel does adopt it (nt_window/bridge.rs apply_event -> configure_compositor_window), so BeginPaint sizes the next backing to the server-reported extent while the backend still compares against the requested one, and Backend::present refuses every later frame (window.width != frame.width). The window then keeps its last pixels for the rest of its life while EndPaint reports success. Boot 2985060 shows mutter configuring hwnd=1 (op=0x102) 138ms after the first paint. Fixed: userspace/probes/windows-compositor/src/extent.rs (ungated decision + 4 unit tests) wired into x11.rs poll_event Configure arm; xvfb integration test xvfb_a_server_resize_is_adopted_so_the_next_frame_still_reaches_the_window. Positive control: fix removed -> 67 passed/1 FAILED; restored -> 68 passed/0 failed (baseline 63). | B3609-frame-presentation |
| KI-0789 | OPEN | COVERAGE | med | A record the desktop refused was dropped silently by the bridge reader, so a frame that never reached a window was indistinguishable from one that did | nt_compositor/queue.rs acknowledge() returned only bool "presented frame"; the reader mapped false to no action, and a drawing submission is unawaited, so a nonzero Ack status had no observer anywhere. Boot 2985060 could not be diagnosed from its log for exactly this reason. Replaced with Settled{Presented,Carried,Refused{opcode,status}} (ungated, tested) and a bounded [WINDOWS-BRIDGE-REFUSED] op/hwnd/seq/status line in nt_compositor/worker.rs::reader. Positive control: Refused folded into Carried -> 2 tests FAILED; restored -> 26 passed. | B3609-frame-presentation |
| KI-0790 | OPEN | INFRA | low | KI-0403 does not reproduce: the Xvfb frame-acceptance test reads the right pixels | Re-ran the exact named test on this host at origin/main + this lane: cargo test -p windows-compositor -> xvfb_protocol_window_frame_and_server_pixels_non_gnome ok, whole suite 63 passed / 0 failed before this lane, 68 / 0 after. The zero-pixel reading KI-0403 records is not present; KI-0403 needs closing or re-evidencing by its owner. Also re-checked KI-0709: its dead-code claim is accurate (capture_region, client_damage, reserve_captured, set_paint_clip_for_current still carry #[allow(dead_code)] and have no non-test callers) but its premise that this is the missing publication is wrong - production PresentGdiWindowRegion goes merge_region -> flush_pending_for_current -> submit_frame and IS reached in boot 2985060 ([WINDOWS-GDI] present milestone fires only from that flush). The output.rs allow(dead_code) comment also cites KI-0708 while the row is KI-0709. | B3609-frame-presentation |
| KI-0796 | OPEN | COVERAGE | med | syscalls native_process_identity integration test broken on main | tests/native_process_identity/tests.rs searches nt_process_create.rs for "sched::live::arm_user_entry(&child"; production now calls arm_user_entry_with_argument(&child, so find().unwrap() panics. Two tests fail (production_hook_precedes_child_publication, executable_parent_slice_rejects_omitted_hook) on unmodified origin/main 897d0821f. | unowned |
| KI-0794 | OPEN | MISSING | high | Keyboard menu tracking never climbs to the window that can carry a menu | nt_window::menu_raw::bar::track_for_current resolves the bar with bar_of(hwnd) on the window the WM_SYSCOMMAND SC_KEYMENU arrived on. The reference walks up to the first ancestor whose style allows a menu before it looks one up, so a bare Alt or an Alt+letter typed into a focused child (Notepad edit control) opens the top-level window bar. Without the climb the command reaches the child, bar_of returns None and nothing opens. B3612 fixed the bare-Alt half in the default window procedure (the key-up SC_KEYMENU now goes to the GA_ROOT ancestor, as the reference does); the WM_SYSCHAR half still needs the climb inside the tracking entry, which is the menu lane owner file. | unowned |
| KI-0851 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | HWND values were allocated per process, so two processes gave two different windows the same handle | Every GUI entry built its window manager with a counter starting at 1, so a second NT process first window carried the first process value. Cross-process resolution by raw HWND (clipboard update posting, input-context owner lookup, window state lookup) picks the FIRST entry whose local numbering contains the value, so it can answer with the wrong process window. Fixed on B3613: handle values come from one system-wide space partitioned into per-owner blocks (ipc win32_window handle_space), the window server own block holds desktop windows. Hosted proof crates/kernel/syscalls/tests/nt_second_instance_hosted.rs two_processes_never_give_two_windows_the_same_handle; positive control - restore the shared counter in WindowManager new_in_block and 4 of 6 tests go red. | Chris Watkins |
| KI-0795 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | Desktop window was the first process own top-level window, so a second instance resolved a foreign HWND | nt_window create offered every created window as the desktop root and NtDesktop publish_root recorded (process, hwnd) first-writer-wins. Consequences measured from the code: (a) a second NT process NtUserGetDesktopWindow answered the FIRST process HWND, which under per-process numbering was also the second process own main window, so a top-level create naming the desktop as parent either failed InvalidParent or silently became a child; (b) GetDC(NULL) was refused in every process but the root owner (KI-0424). Reference: the desktop window belongs to the desktop object and every attached process resolves the same handle. Fixed on B3613: NtDesktop owns its window, handle drawn from the window server block, established on first request and answered to every later caller; dispatch maps a desktop parent to no parent; GetDC and the rect query answer the desktop rectangle without any process window record. | Chris Watkins |
| KI-0852 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | Named-object creation always reports STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_COLLISION and never reads OBJ_OPENIF | Every named create/publish caller (nt_dispatch CreateSymbolicLink/CreateEvent/CreateSection, nt_wine_unix section-map/CreateEvent/CreateMutex/CreateSemaphore) maps NamedObjectState Existing to STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_COLLISION (error class). The reference returns the existing object with STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_EXISTS (informational success) when OBJ_OPENIF is set, which the Win32 Create* wrappers always set; oxide never reads the attribute at all. Masked for Win32 callers because the wrappers return the handle regardless and both statuses map to ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS, but a raw NT caller testing NT_SUCCESS sees a failure, and a caller that deliberately omits OBJ_OPENIF is silently handed the existing object instead of being refused. |  |
| KI-0797 | OPEN | MISSING | med | No per-session NT object namespace: Local\\ and Global\\ resolve nowhere and \\Sessions has no children | The namespace seeds \\Sessions as an empty directory; nothing creates \\Sessions\\<id>\\BaseNamedObjects or resolves the Local\\ / Global\\ prefixes, so every session shares one \\BaseNamedObjects. Harmless for two instances of one interactive application, which real Windows also places in one session namespace, but a service and a second logon session would collide where Windows isolates them. |  |
| KI-0798 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | FLS index table is one system-wide list, not one per process | nt_fls.rs keeps a single static INDEXES Vec for the whole system while the values live per thread in the TEB. Fiber-local storage indexes are per-process in the reference: a second process RtlFlsAlloc is numbered around the first process allocations, one process RtlFlsFree invalidates an index another process still uses, and nothing releases a process indexes when it exits, so the table only grows. Found auditing the second-instance path (B3613); not on the Notepad startup path but a real cross-process leak of per-process state. |  |
| KI-0799 | OPEN | DEFECT | low | Compositor focus record sets a process foreground without clearing the others | nt_window/bridge.rs sets entry.foreground from the desktop focus record for the entry the record names, but unlike the dispatch path it does not clear the flag on every other entry. Two processes can then both be foreground and route_hardware_key / route_hardware_rel / the input-context foreground-thread answer take the FIRST such entry in GUI order. Only reachable when the desktop does not own physical input, which is why a second instance under GNOME does not hit it. |  |
| KI-0800 | OPEN | COVERAGE | med | native_process_identity source-text test asserts a call the production file no longer makes | crates/kernel/syscalls/tests/native_process_identity/tests.rs production_hook_precedes_child_publication searches nt_process_create.rs for "sched::live::arm_user_entry(&child"; the file now calls arm_user_entry_with_argument, so find() returns None and the test panics on unwrap. Pre-existing on main, unrelated to B3613 (that branch does not touch nt_process_create.rs); the ordering contract it means to pin is currently unchecked. |  |
| KI-0853 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | The Notepad window title bar renders empty on the live session; the kernel publication path is sound | Measured on target/windows-notepad-acceptance/screen-3269418-locate-probe.ppm: the main window frame carries a close button and no name. Disproved the one-unit truncation theory: every reader on the path is unit-based and none stops on a zero byte. crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_wine_window.rs read_optional_unicode_string divides the byte Length by two and reads u16 at a time; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_wine_window/dispatch.rs NtWindowCall::SetText reads u16 units until a zero unit; ipc win32_window state.rs set_text/text keep and return the whole Vec<u16>; nt_window/bridge.rs snapshot converts the whole slice with from_utf16_lossy and refuses a payload holding a zero. The UART for that run shows WM_SETTEXT (msg=0xc) reaching hwnd=1 at 68.880 under the send selector and again at 68.882 under the default-window-procedure selector 0x29e, which is the arm that calls NtService::SetWindowText, and nt_window/dispatch.rs publishes Opcode::Title after that call succeeds, so a title was set and published. Found and fixed one real divergence on the presentation side: the compositor published only _NET_WM_NAME, while the reference also sets WM_NAME and WM_ICON_NAME through XSetWMName/XSetWMIconName; userspace/probes/windows-compositor/src/x11.rs publish_title now sets all three. Whether that alone restores the name is unverified: this lane may not boot. Remaining suspects if it does not: the interned UTF8_STRING/_NET_WM_NAME atoms, and the create-time Title publish carrying an empty name when the window is created with no name. | B3614-system-metrics |
| KI-0801 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | NtUserMessageCall published the 4-byte BOOL source-encoding argument as a full 8-byte stack word, so caller residue in the high half marked a Unicode sender ANSI; the runtime then measured the wide message string with byte semantics | Boot trace: hwnd=0x100001 units=7 "Notepad" at create, then units=1 bytes=1 text=U after SetWindowTextW("Untitled - Notepad") - UTF-16LE 55 00 6E 00 measured as a byte string is exactly 1. Fixed by masking the slot to its low 32 bits in nt_wine_window/message_call_abi.rs::tail; 4 hosted tests (2 new), positive control red 1-vs-18 then green. | chris@watkinslabs.com |
| KI-0854 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | ShowWindow published only visibility: a newly shown top-level window was never raised to the top of its band or activated, so an owned dialog was mapped wherever the display already had it in the stack and competed with its owner for the same screen area. | Reference show_window() ends every show that reaches its positioning step with SetWindowPos(hwnd, HWND_TOP, ..., swp), adding SWP_NOZORDER\|SWP_NOACTIVATE only for WS_CHILD and for the no-activate/minimize commands. nt_window/dispatch.rs Show arm called only bridge::publish_visibility_current; nothing ever called publish_position_current on a show, although the compositor already implements Position (restack ABOVE for insertion 0, _NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW for activate) and position/live.rs commit() already publishes exactly that shape for an explicit SetWindowPos. The fixture include!s src/nt_window/dispatch.rs into a hosted module and its hand-written stubs have drifted from the real module surface. | KI-0805 | OPEN | COVERAGE | med | The menu face measurement is proven hosted on both sides but nothing yet shows the redirect completing in a running guest | B3619 measures the menu face through a kernel-consumed font-backend query launched from the first menu bar paint and redraws the bar when the advances land. Both halves are pinned hosted: the backend table summed the kernel way reproduces the extent the same run measures whole against the shipped face, and the queue test shows the measurement taking the backend ahead of the runs that wait on it. What no hosted check can show is the round trip itself - that the redirect installs on the painting thread, the completion publishes, and the redraw produces a second bar frame laid out on the real advances. Until a boot shows it, a menu bar drawn on the average advance is indistinguishable from one whose measurement never completed. |  |
| KI-0806 | OPEN | INFRA | low | Five known-issue ids name two different rows each, so issues.sh --check is red on main and --show/--fix reach only the first | tools/issues.sh --check on the untouched main tree at 3864edf15 reports duplicate id for KI-0794, KI-0796, KI-0797, KI-0798 and KI-0799: two parallel lanes minted ids from their own copy of the ledger. --show KI-0797 answers the object-namespace row while --query grep=menu lists the menu-width row under the same id, and --fix would flip whichever comes first. B3619 renumbered the two rows it closed (to KI-0803/KI-0804) before flipping them; the remaining three pairs are untouched. |  |

| KI-0811 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | Window server decoration is not taken off the surface a decorated top level presents | Reference computes the presented surface as the window rectangle minus the decoration the window manager draws (a title bar and a resize border), and its own nonclient painter then draws the whole frame into that surface. B3621 takes the manager-drawn styles out of the frame the window draws and reserves (ipc win32_window/nonclient_frame.rs own_styles + syscall nt_compositor::managed), which puts the same pixels on screen for the styles involved but leaves the caption band and the sizing border undrawn by us. Closing it means presenting the visible rectangle rather than the window rectangle: a bridge geometry change that needs a boot to verify. | B3621 |
| KI-0812 | OPEN | MISSING | high | Default WM_NCPAINT draws no caption bar, system menu or nonclient scrollbar band | Reference nonclient paint draws, in order, the outer edge, the frame, the caption (icon, text, buttons), the menu bar, the client edge and the scrollbars. B3621 adds the outer edge, the frame and the client edge beside the existing menu bar; the caption is drawn only by an explicitly invoked DrawCaptionTemp ordinal that nothing calls from WM_NCPAINT (nt_window/caption/live.rs, reached only from the ordinal table). While a decorated window keeps its caption from the window manager this is invisible on the desktop; an undecorated captioned window has no caption at all. | B3621 |
| KI-0814 | OPEN | INFRA | high | make hosted-gate is red on main: elf-load fails -D warnings with an unused import and four unnecessary unsafe blocks, blocking every branch push | On origin/main 02f9717b1: crates/kernel/exec/src/pe_startup.rs:6 unused import VmaBacking; crates/kernel/exec/src/process_env/processor_features.rs:133,138,145,151 unnecessary unsafe around __cpuid_count, which the pinned toolchain now exposes as a safe intrinsic. 5 errors compiling elf-load lib; every lane had to pass SKIP_HOSTED_GATE=1. After the fix hosted-check reports PASS, 180 crates type-check in isolation, and both kernel arches --check clean. | chris |
| KI-0815 | OPEN | INFRA | high | make feature-gate-x86 is red on clean main: seven ARM-only items in the processor-feature vector are dead code in the x86 kernel build | Measured on untouched origin/main 3f7960657 with a clean worktree: feature-gate-x86 exits 2 with PF_ARM_VFP_32_REGISTERS_AVAILABLE, PF_ARM_NEON_INSTRUCTIONS_AVAILABLE, PF_ARM_DIVIDE_INSTRUCTION_AVAILABLE, PF_ARM_64BIT_LOADSTORE_ATOMIC, PF_ARM_FMAC_INSTRUCTIONS_AVAILABLE, PF_ARM_V8_INSTRUCTIONS_AVAILABLE never used and function aarch64_features never used, in crates/kernel/exec/src/process_env/processor_features.rs. The module deliberately keeps the mapping ungated so it is testable without a target build, so the fix is not a blanket cfg: the published numbering is an ABI table whose entries are not all consumed by every target build, and the right owner for that distinction has to be decided. Not caused by B3622: the same gate on that branch reports these 7 and no others, against 12 on clean main. | unassigned |
| KI-0816 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | Six unreachable items in the syscalls crate, previously masked by the elf-load build failure — at least one is an access check that is never performed | Revealed once elf-load compiled again (B3622): feature-gate-x86 now reaches the syscalls crate and reports nt_keyed_event.rs:36 granted_access never used; nt_access.rs:54,56,57 EVENT_QUERY_STATE, EVENT_ALL_ACCESS and the EVENT access map never used; nt_wine_window/nonclient_raw.rs:19 MOUSE_BYTES never used; nt_wine_unwind/answer.rs:34 UnsupportedMachine never constructed. granted_access and the EVENT access map are the same shape as the section access-mask defect fixed earlier in this campaign: an admission decision written but never consulted, so every caller is admitted unchecked. Each needs its call site restored or a reason it does not exist, not deletion. These were invisible while the build stopped at elf-load. | unassigned |
| KI-0817 | OPEN | PERF | high | First registry transaction of an NT process costs ~0.42 s before any work is done | Measured from a live guest log: the first NT registry round trip of a Notepad startup (ord 0x12 NtOpenKey at t=75.911) took 0.416 s, the largest single gap in the 4.30 s PE-startup window; the same call later in the same startup costs 1-5 ms, and in a second startup of the same binary the whole OpenKey set costs 0.021 s total. The registry owner spawns one std thread per accepted connection (userspace/probes/windows-registry/src/service.rs serve_listener) and the launcher hands the process an already-connected endpoint at exec, so the cost is daemon-side: worker-thread spawn plus first-touch page faults on the accept path. Not addressed by B3624 (which removed the per-set whole-database commit and the per-call ordinal trace). Suspect the same page-fault-driven spawn cost as B1382; needs the daemon side measured, not the kernel side. | B3624-pe-startup-latency |
| KI-0818 | OPEN | DEFECT | low | NT status trace reports an API-normal negative answer as a refusal, 94 times per PE startup | status_reportable (crates/kernel/syscalls/src/nt_dispatch.rs) treats every status with severity 0xC0000000 as a refusal worth a console line. NtAreMappedFilesTheSame answers STATUS_NOT_SAME_DEVICE (0xC00000D4) as its normal negative result, so one measured Notepad startup emitted 94 `[WINDOWS-NT-STATUS] service=0x255 status=0xC00000D4` lines describing nothing wrong (plus 30 more for service 0x120 and 36 for 0x0b, all STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_NOT_FOUND / STATUS_OBJECT_PATH_NOT_FOUND probes a loader makes on purpose). At the ~1.4 ms per-record floor measured in the same log that is ~0.13 s of synchronous console writes per startup, and it buries real refusals. Fix is the same shape B3624 applied to the ordinal trace: report a distinct (service, status) pair once, or exclude statuses a caller uses as an answer. | B3624-pe-startup-latency |
| KI-0819 | OPEN | INFRA | high | The stack gate has been unable to run at all, and on its first working run reports 334 chains above budget, the deepest at 20168 bytes | Measured on B3625, the first branch where the kernel builds cleanly enough for make stack-gate to reach its analysis: 334 entries marked NEW, led by oxide_syscall_entry 20168, nt_dispatch::dispatch_service 20136, kmain::entry::kernel_init 20064, and including nt_window::menu_raw::bar::track_for_current 13000 and nt_window::hardware::live::enter 13008 on the Windows path. The gate could not run while elf-load failed to compile, so none of these has ever been assessed. This matters more here than a lint would: the multi-session boot-corruption campaign resolved as a kernel-stack overflow scribbling the adjacent heap, so an unmeasured stack budget is the failure mode this project has already paid for once. Needs triage against the real per-task budget before any of it is allowlisted. | unassigned |
| KI-0858 | OPEN | MISSING | med | NtUserCallOneParam_GetDeskPattern always answers an empty pattern: no SystemParametersInfo action writes the session desktop pattern | The session desktop pattern owner (ipc UserSettings::set_desk_pattern) has no caller: the SPI settings owner (ipc::win32_sysparams::action) defines no SPI_SETDESKPATTERN entry, so the pattern can never become non-empty and the query answers 0 for every caller. The query itself is correct for an unset pattern (B3623). | B3623 |
| KI-0820 | OPEN | MISSING | med | NtUserCallTwoParam_SetIMECompositionRect answers FALSE for every window: the compositor protocol carries no composition-rectangle opcode | The composition rectangle belongs to the display driver, which positions the composition and candidate windows over it. The compositor wire protocol opcodes are Close/Configure/Create/Damage/Destroy/Focus/Geometry/Key/Pointer/Position/Text/Title/Visibility - none carries a composition rectangle. The call validates the window and the rectangle and then answers FALSE, which is the reference answer for a driver with no entry point for it (B3623). | B3623 |
| KI-0856 | OPEN | MISSING | low | NtUserCallOneParam_EnableThunkLock retains the callback but no 16-bit thunk-lock path calls it | The reference stores the callback and invokes it from the user lock when 16-bit code holds it. The kernel retains the callback per process (GuiEntry::thunk_lock) and has no 16-bit thunk path, so the stored callback is never invoked. The call answers 0, as the reference does (B3623). | B3623 |
| KI-0857 | OPEN | DEFECT | low | NtUserCallNoParam_GetDialogBaseUnits clamps each base unit away from zero; the reference does not | The reference packs muldiv(cell, system_dpi, 96) per axis with no clamp, so a zero character cell answers zero. This kernel clamps each axis to at least one pixel because a zero base unit divides by zero in every dialog-unit conversion the client then makes. Deliberate, pinned by a hosted test, and diverges only for a zero cell (B3623). | B3623 |
| KI-0821 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | QEMU guest keeps the last cursor shape from the region the pointer passed through, and the host cursor is drawn over the guest area | Reported from a live session: dragging the mouse across the QEMU window leaves the pointer showing whatever shape was last set in a region it crossed - crossing a window corner leaves the resize cursor in force outside that corner. Found by lane B3623 (win32u call-parameter multiplexers), out of its file ownership; needs a lane on the cursor set/leave path and the QEMU display cursor policy. |  |

| KI-0872 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | Ptrace interrupt publishes a stop before the tracee parks and sends SIGSTOP instead of a trap request | 101_ptrace/sig.rs::interrupt stores stop_pending, stop_code and ptrace_siginfo on the tracer, then sends process SIGSTOP. Signal delivery reports a bare signal stop and overwrites the event code. Required interrupt arms task jobctl TRAP_STOP without changing signal queues or disturbing an existing non-listening ptrace stop. Return-to-user work currently only sees queued signals, not pending jobctl traps. live/stop.rs also reuses the original stop code and kind on a retrap. Source audit during KI-0871; runtime causality for GNOME remains unverified. | B3630-paint-region-collapse |
| KI-0873 | OPEN | DEFECT | high | Attaching to an already group-stopped task does not transition it into a ptrace stop | 101_ptrace::attach only publishes trace metadata and an optional attach signal. The signal publisher intentionally does not wake stopped tasks for SIGSTOP, so a target already in a job-control stop remains there. Attachment must arm the stop trap, wake the target through the stopped-to-traced transition, and wait for that transition before returning. SEIZE also requires this transition for a group-stopped target. Existing live/stop.rs retrap branch retains its original StopKind and stop code, so merely arming TRAP_STOP is insufficient. Source audit during KI-0871; no new boot. | B3630-paint-region-collapse |

| KI-0890 | OPEN | DEFECT | med | Default mouse activation bypasses parent handling and caption-specific behavior | Dispatcher audit: raw_callback::message_call sends default WM_MOUSEACTIVATE to DefaultWindowProc; canonical default_window_proc has no WM_MOUSEACTIVATE arm and returns0. The source forwards a child request to its parent, honors a nonzero result, then returns MA_NOACTIVATE for caption left-button-down or MA_ACTIVATE otherwise. Retrieval treats0 as activate, so ordinary client activation can work while parent veto/eat and caption semantics are absent. No live post-hit-test-fix verification yet. | B3630-paint-region-collapse |
